Crispy Crunchy Baby Corn Favourite
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Bite-sized bursts of flavor, perfectly fried to golden perfection, offering a satisfying crunch with every munch.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 6-10 minutes Cook: 6-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 10-
• 2 baby corns, blanched 1¼ teaspoon red chilli powder + to sprinkle ¼ teaspoon turmeric powder
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder ½ teaspoon crushed black peppercorns
• 1 tablespoon oil + for deep frying
• 1 cup refined flour (maida) ½ cup cornflour A pinch of baking soda
• 1 cup cornflakes Chaat masala to sprinkle Green chutney to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take baby corns in a bowl, add 1 teaspoon red chilli powder, turmeric powder, garam masala powder, crushed black peppercorns, ginger-garlic paste and salt and stir until combined.
2. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai Take refined flour, cornflour, baking soda, remaining red chilli powder and a pinch of salt in another bowl.
3. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
4. Add ½ cup water and 1 tablespoon oil and stir until combined to make a thick batter.
5. Crush the cornflakes and add to the batter and stir until combined.
6. Dip baby corns in this batter and slide into hot oil and deep fry on medium heat till golden brown.
7. Drain on absorbent paper.
8. Sprinkle red chilli powder and chaat masala over them.
9. Place in a serving basket and serve warm with green chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Crispy Spinach and Coriander Bites Mix
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Kothimbir wadi is a well-loved Maharashtrian snack and here we have made it healthier with the addition of spinach

Cuisine: Maharashtrian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 21-25 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup chopped spinach leaves
• 2 cups chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 2 tablespoons yogurt
• 2 teaspoons oil + to grease ½ teaspoon mustard seeds ¼ teaspoon asafoetida
• 1 teaspoon white sesame seeds (safed til)
• 1 tablespoon scraped fresh coconut + for garnish

Method
1. Warm 4 cups water in a steamer.
2. Mix coriander leaves, spinach and besan in a bowl.
3. Add turmeric powder, ginger green chilli paste, salt, dried mango powder and yogurt and stir until combined.
4. Add little water and make a dropping consistency mixture.
5. Grease a thali with some oil.
6. Transfer the mixture in it.
7. Tap the thali to level the mixture.
8. Place the thali in the steamer and steam for 12-15 minutes on medium heat.
9. Release the steamed mixture from sides of the thali with a palette knife and invert the thali on the worktop.
10. Trim the edges of the wadi.
11. Cut into squares.
12. Warm 2 teaspoons oil in a non-stick pan, add mustard seeds and let them splutter.
13. Add asafoetida, white sesame seeds and wadis and cook on low heat for 2-3 minutes.
14. Flip and cook until golden brown on both the sides.
15. Add coconut and cook for few seconds.
16. Switch off heat.
17. Transfer the vadis onto a serving platter, garnish with scraped coconut and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Wholesome Dal Amboli
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Discover the satisfying flavors of Wholesome Dal Amboli, a classic South Indian comfort food. This dish combines fluffy, fermented rice pancakes with a flavorful dal made from lentils, tomatoes, and aromatic...

Cuisine: Maharashtrian Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy

Ingredients
• 1 cup short grain rice ½ cup pressed rice (poha) ¼ cup split pigeon peas (toovar dal) ¼ cup split skinless green gram (dhuli moong dal) ¼ cup split green gram with skin (chilkewali moong dal) ¼ cup split Bengal gram (chana dal) ¼ cup split red lentils (masoor dal)
• 1 tablespoon flax seeds
• 2 teaspoons fenugreek seeds
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take rice in a bowl.
2. Add pressed rice, pigeon peas, green gram, green gram with skin, Bengal gram, red lentils, flax seeds, and fenugreek seeds.Wash for 2-3 times.
3. Add 3 cups buttermilk and soak for 6-8 hours.
4. Transfer the mixture in a grinder and grind coarsely.
5. Place in a bowl, add remaining buttermilk and stir until combined.
6. Set aside to ferment for 8-10 hours.
7. Add salt and stir until combined.
8. Warm a cast iron tawa.
9. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
10. Dip onion half in oil and grease tawa.
11. Pour a ladleful of batter, drizzle oil, cover and cook for 3-4 minutes on medium heat.
12. Flip and cook for 2-3 minutes more.
13. Serve warm with coconut chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Simple Dal Baati Churma
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

This trio of sweet and savoury dishes on a platter is among the finest and most popular offerings from Rajasthan. Served with generous amounts of ghee is metaphorically a way of showing love to the...

Cuisine: Rajasthani Course: Main Course- Vegetarin Cook: 35-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• Baatis
• 2 cups whole wheat flour (atta)
• 4 tablespoons semolina ½ cup pure ghee + to deep fry + to serve
• 1 cup milk ¾ cup powdered sugar ¼ teaspoon green cardamom powder
• 0 cashewnuts, chopped
• 0 almonds, chopped
• 0 raisins Dal
• 2 cups cooked split red lentils (masoor dal) 2-
• 3 tablespoons oil
• 2 teaspoons cumin seeds
• 3 green chillies, chopped 6-
• 8 garlic cloves, chopped
• 1 tablespoon red chilli powder
• 2 teaspoons garam masala powder
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Preheat oven to 180° C.
2. To make baatis, take whole wheat flour in a parat.
3. Add semolina, salt, ½ cup ghee and stir until combined.
4. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
5. Add 1¼ cups water knead into stiff dough.
6. Divide the dough into small equal portions and roll into balls and arrange them on a baking tray.
7. Bake them in the preheated oven for 15-20 minutes.
8. Dip the baked baatis in melted ghee and let them soak for 5-10 minutes.
9. To make churma, mix whole wheat flour and semolina in a parat.
10. Add ½ cup melted ghee and stir until combined.
11. Add milk and knead into a stiff dough.
12. Divide the dough into equal portions and shape into cylinders.
13. Warm sufficient ghee in a kadai and deep-fry the balls on medium heat, till they are golden brown and crisp.
14. Drain on absorbent paper and cool.
15. Place in a grinder jar and grind coarsely Add powdered sugar, cardamom powder, cashew nuts, almonds and raisins and mix.
16. The churma is ready.
17. To make dal, heat oil in deep non-stick pan and add cumin seeds.
18. Once they start to change the colour, add green chillies, garlic and fry gently for 30 seconds, Add red chilli powder, garam masala powder, tomato and fry gently till tomatoes turn soft and pulpy.
19. Add boiled dal, salt, 2 cups water and stir until combined.
20. Cook on low heat for 5 minutes.
21. Drizzle ghee on dal, baati, churma and serve warm with onion and green chillies.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Comfort Dal Dhokli
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Comfort Dal Dhokli is an authentic Guajarati dish which is luscious in flavour and tastes best when served hot. A family favourite, it appeases all age groups

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Main Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 1/
• 2 cups split pigeon peas (tuvar dal) 1/
• 1 tablespoon raw peanuts
• 2 tablespoons ghee
• 1 teaspoon mustard seeds 1/
• 4 teaspoon fenugreek seeds (methi dana) 1/
• 4 teaspoon asafoetida
• 2 dried red chillies, broken 16-
• 8 curry leaves
• 1 teaspoon ginger-green chilli paste
• 1 teaspoon grated jaggery
• 1 kokam petal, soaked in water for
• 5 minutes
• 1 tsp lemon juice+ for drizzling
• 1 small onion, finely chopped Fresh coriander leaves, chopped to garnish Dhokli 3/
• 4 cup whole wheat flour (atta) 1/
• 4 teaspoon turmeric powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon red chilli powder A pinch of asafoetida 1/
• 2 teaspoon ginger-green chilli paste to apply + teaspoons Oil

Method
1. Pressure cook pigeon peas in some water with ¼ teaspoon turmeric powder, salt and peanuts till 3-4 whistles are given out.
2. Open the lid when the pressure is completely released.
3. To prepare dhokli, combine wheat flour and gram flour in a bowl.
4. Add salt, ¼ teaspoon turmeric powder, chilli powder, asafoetida, ginger-garlic paste and sufficient water and knead into a semi-soft dough.
5. Add 2 teaspoons oil and knead again.
6. Warm ghee in a deep non-stick pan, add mustard seeds and let them splutter.
7. Add fenugreek seeds, asafoetida, broken dried red chillies, curry leaves and cooked pigeon peas and stir until combined.
8. Add 1½ cups water and cook until the mixture comes to a boil.
9. Cover and cook for 2-3 minutes.
10. Divide the dough into equal portions, apply some oil on each portion and roll out into large thin discs.
11. Move onto the worktop and cut each disc into diamonds.
12. These are dhoklis.
13. Add ginger-green chilli paste, and salt to pigeon peas mixture and stir until combined.
14. Add dhokli pieces to the pan and mix.
15. Add jaggery and kokum petal, mix, cover and cook for 10-15 minutes or till dhokli pieces are done.
16. Switch off heat, add lemon juice and stir until combined.
17. Spoon into a serving bowl, top with some onion and coriander leaves, drizzle some lemon juice and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Spiced Dal Soya Parantha
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Paranthas stuffed with a highly flavoured and nutritious mixture of chana dal and soya chunks will leave you asking for more and more and…

Cuisine: Indian Course: Breads Prep: 2-2.30 hour Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 4 cup Split Bengal gram (chana dal), soaked and boiled
• 1 cup Soya chunks, soaked in hot water
• 0 grams Readymade whole wheat flour dough
• 1 tbsp + for basting Ghee
• 1 teaspoon Fennel seeds (saunf)
• 1 teaspoon Cumin seeds (jeera)
• 1 teaspoon Carom seeds (ajwain)
• 1 tablespoon Ginger, finely chopped 2-
• 3 Green chillies, finely chopped
• 1 teaspoon Red chilli powder
• 1 tablespoon Coriander powder
• 1 teaspoon Cumin powder
• 1 teaspoon Garam masala powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Black salt
• 1 Dried mango powder (amchur)
• 1 teaspoon Black peppercorns, crushed 10-
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm ghee in a non-stick pan, add fennel seeds, cumin seeds and carom seeds, mix and fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add ginger and green chillies, mix and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
4. Add Bengal gram and soya chunks and mix.
5. Add red chilli powder, coriander powder, cumin powder, garam masala powder, black salt, dried mango powder and crushed peppercorns, mix and cook for 3-4 minutes.
6. Transfer the mixture into a blender jar and blend to a coarse paste.
7. Transfer the paste into a bowl.
8. Chop coriander leaves, add to the bowl and stir until combined.
9. Add salt and mix again.
10. Warm a non-stick tawa.
11. Divide the dough into equal portions and roll each portion into a ball.
12. Roll out each portion to a disc, place a portion of dal-soya mixture in the centre, bring edges together, press to seal and roll into a ball.
13. Dust each stuffed ball with dry flour and roll out into a parantha.
14. Place the paranthas on the hot tawa, one at a time, and cook, basting with ghee, till evenly cooked and golden on both sides.
15. Move onto individual serving plates and serve warm with your choice of pickle.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Desserts Cook: 0 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet

Ingredients
• 10-
• 2 dates ¼ cup cocoa powder
• 2 teaspoons vanilla essence ½ cup tender coconut water
• 0 grams tender coconut water

Method
1. Transfer dates in a bowl.
2. Add sufficient water and soak for 2-3 hours.
3. Deseed and transfer in a grinder jar.
4. Add cocoa powder, vanilla essence, tender coconut water, tender coconut flesh and grind to a fine mixture.
5. Place in an air tight container, close the lid and freeze for 6-8 hours.
6. Scoop and serve chilled.

Comfort Deconstructed Vada Pav
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A modern twist on the iconic Indian street food, featuring separated elements like spiced potato patties, bread, and spicy chutney for a unique culinary experience. This recipe is from Food Food TV...

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 6-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 8 slices of French baguette, toasted with butter till golden
• 1 teaspoon oil ½ teaspoon mustard seeds
• 5 curry leaves 1½ tablespoons ginger-garlic-green chilli-coriander leaves paste ½ teaspoon turmeric powder 5-
• 1 cup salted boondi Chives, halved for garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm oil in a deep non-stick pan, add mustard seeds and curry leaves.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Once the mustard seeds splutter, add ginger-garlic-green chilli-coriander leaves paste.
4. Saute on medium heat till oil separates.
5. Add turmeric powder and stir until combined.
6. Add sweet potatoes and stir until combined.
7. Add salt and fry gently for a minute.
8. Switch off heat.
9. Place 2 baguette slices on a worktop.
10. Spread some green chutney over them.
11. Sprinkle vada pav chutney on top.
12. Spread some cooked mixture in a dome shape on top.
13. Take boondis in a bowl and roll the prepared bread slices, upside down, in it till well coated with boondi.
14. Make more deconstructed vada pavs in the same way.
15. Transfer deconstructed vada pavs on a serving platter.
16. Serve at once, garnished with chives.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Classic Double Decker Parantha
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Just one such parantha will satiate you completely stuffed as it is with masaledar rajma and paneer

Cuisine: Indian Course: Breads Prep: 8-10 hour Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup Red kidney beans (rajma), soaked overnight, boiled and mashed
• 1 teaspoon Garam masala powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Red chilli powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon Dried mango powder (amchur_) 1/
• 4 teaspoon Chaat masala 1/
• 2 teaspoon Turmeric powder
• 1 cup Cottage cheese (paneer), grated 1/
• 2 cup Mozzarella cheese, grated 1/
• 2 cup Cheddar cheese, grated
• 1 tablespoon Fresh mint leaves, finely chopped to sprinkle Chaat masala to shallow fry Ghee grams Yogurt
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take kidney beans in a bowl, add garam masala powder, red chilli powder, dried mango powder, chaat masala and turmeric powder and stir until combined.
2. Add onion salt and stir until combined.
3. Take cottage cheese in another bowl, add mozzarella cheese, cheddar cheese, mint leaves and chaat masala and stir until combined.
4. Divide the dough into 4 small equal portions, 4 slightly bigger equal portions and 4 bigger equal portions.
5. To prepare the middle layer rotis, sprinkle flour on a worktop, take each small portion of dough and roll it thinly.
6. Warm a non-stick tawa, put this roti on it and cook, turning sides, till light brown specs appear on both sides.
7. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
8. To prepare the first layer rotis, sprinkle flour on the worktop, take each bigger portion and roll thinly, ensuring that it is bigger than the middle layer roti.
9. To prepare the third layer rotis, sprinkle flour and take each slightly big portion of dough and roll thinly into a size that is bigger than the middle layer roti but smaller than the first layer roti.
10. Take the first layer roti, spread spoonful of rajma mixture all over it.
11. Place the middle layer cooked roti over it.
12. Spread the cheese mixture all over it.
13. Cover with third layer roti.
14. Press completely.
15. Warm the non-stick tawa again, place a parantha on it and cook, on medium heat, turning sides, till golden brown specs appear on both sides.
16. Drizzle ghee and continue to cook for 30 seconds on each side.
17. Make more paranthe similarly.
18. Keep each parantha on separate serving plates and serve warm with yogurt.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Simple Dum ka Tinda
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Scooped out tindas stuffed with a spicy paneer mixture and cooked in a rich gravy

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 9 Apple gourds (tinda)
• 1 teaspoon Ginger, chopped
• 2 Green chillies, finely chopped
• 1 cup Cottage cheese (paneer), grated
• 4 bunch Fresh coriander leaves 3-
• 4 tablespoons Sunflower oil
• 1 teaspoon Cumin seeds
• 2 medium Onions, finely chopped
• 1 1/
• 2 tablespoons Ginger-garlic paste
• 3 medium Tomatoes, chopped
• 2 tablespoons Coriander powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Cumin powder
• 1 teaspoon Garam masala powder
• 2 teaspoons Dried mango powder (amchur) 3/
• 4 cups Cashewnuts, boiled
• 2 inches Ginger, cut into thin strips 2-
• 3 Green chillies, cut into thin strips
• 1 medium Onion, cut into
• 1 inch cubes and layers separated
• 6 Big green chillies, stems removed and halved 1/
• 4 teaspoon Turmeric powder 1/
• 4 cup Khoya, grated
• 1 Lemon to garnish Fresh mint sprig
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Peel the apple gourds.
2. Cut off a thin slice from the top and reserve them.
3. Scoop out the inner pulp with a scooper.
4. Take ginger, green chillies, cottage cheese, 1 tsp red chilli powder and salt in a bowl.
5. Blend the scooped portion of apple gourds to a fine paste and add to the cottage cheese mixture.
6. Chop coriander leaves and add and stir until combined.
7. Stuff the apple gourds with this mixture.
8. Warm a non-stick grill pan, place the stuffed apple gourds on it with the reserved sliced off tops.
9. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
10. Add 2 tbsps sunflower oil and grill, turning sides, till golden on all sides and till grill marks appear.
11. Warm remaining oil in a non-stick kadai, add cumin seeds and fry gently till it changes colour.
12. Add onions and fry gently till it turns golden brown.
13. Add ginger-garlic paste and fry gently for a minute.
14. Add tomatoes and salt.
15. Cook until tomatoes turn pulpy.
16. Sprinkle a little salt over the grilled apple gourds.
17. Add coriander powder, 1 tsp red chilli powder, cumin powder, garam masala powder, 1 tsp dried mango powder in the kadai and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
18. Add 10-12 cashewnuts and fry gently till oil separates.
19. Switch off heat.
20. Let the mixture cool down.Transfer the mixture into blender jar and blend to a fine paste.
21. In same kadai, add ginger strips, green chilli strips, onion and halved big green chillies and fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
22. Add the apple gourds and cook on medium heat for 4-5 minutes.
23. Add remaining red chilli powder, turmeric powder and remaining dried mango powder.
24. Add sufficient water.
25. Cover and cook on medium heat for 4-5 minutes.
26. Add sufficient water and add the ground paste.
27. Add khoya, remaining cashewnuts, remaining ginger strips and juice of ½ lemon.
28. Cover and cook on medium heat for 4-5 minutes.
29. Transfer the apple gourds into a serving bowl along with the chillies and gravy.
30. Serve warm garnished with cashewnuts and mint sprig.

Easy Lentil Toss
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Elevate your salad game with our 'Lentil Salad,' a fantastic high protein recipe that’s both quick to prepare and loaded with nutrients. Whether you’re looking for a quick lunch or a healthy side...

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Salads Cook: 4-5 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 2 cups boiled whole red lentils (akkha masoor)
• 3 tablespoons olive oil
• 2 teaspoons finely chopped garlic
• 1 teaspoon chopped ginger
• 2 green chillies, chopped
• 1 tablespoon lemon juice
• 1 medium carrot, peeled ½ medium red capsicum ½ medium yellow capsicum ½ small English cucumber 20-
• 5 fresh mint leaves
• 2 teaspoons honey A large pinch of chaat masala Roasted peanuts for garnish Fresh mint sprigs for garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm olive oil in pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add garlic, ginger, green chillies and onion and fry gently till translucent.
4. Add salt, black pepper powder, cumin powder and red chilli powder and fry gently for 1 minute.
5. Add ¼ cup water and bring to a boil.
6. Switch the heat off, add the lemon juice, mix and set aside to cool completely.
7. Cut the carrot into ½ in cubes, cut the red capsicum and yellow capsicum into ½ inch cubes as well.
8. And cut the cucumber into ½ inch pieces.
9. Transfer the chopped vegetables into a large bowl, add the boiled lentils, mint leaves and cooled onion mixture.
10. Stir in the honey and add chaat masala and stir until combined till combined.
11. Garnish with roasted peanuts and mint sprigs and serve.

Wholesome Energy Bar
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Fuel up with these energy bars packed with a myriad of nutritious ingredients, delivering a powerhouse of energy in every bite.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 1.30-2 hour Cook: 6-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup oats
• 2 tablespoons chopped almonds
• 2 tablespoons chopped cashewnuts
• 2 tablespoons chopped walnuts
• 2 tablespoons chopped pistachios
• 2 tablespoons flax seeds
• 2 tablespoons pumpkin seeds
• 2 tablespoons sunflower seeds ¼ cup chopped cranberries ½ cup date paste ⅓ cup honey ¼ teaspoon salt
• 1 teaspoon butter + for greasing
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm a non-stick pan, add oats and dry roast on medium heat for 3-4 minutes.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Move onto a plate and set aside to cool.
4. In a same pan add almonds, cashewnuts, walnuts, pistachios, flax seeds, pumpkin seeds and sunflower seeds and dry roast on medium heat for 2-3 minutes, Move onto another plate and set aside to cool.
5. Grease a barfi tray with butter.
6. Take cranberries in a mixing bowl, add oats, mixed nuts and seeds and date paste and stir until combined.
7. Gradually add honey and stir until combined.
8. Transfer this mixture onto the greased barfi tray, spread it evenly and keep it in refrigerator for 1½ hours.
9. Cut into required shape, demould and serve.

Quick Farali Paniyaram
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Quick Farali Paniyaram is a savory South Indian snack made especially during fasting days or festivals like Navratri.

Cuisine: South Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• ½ cup rajgira flour ¼ cup potato, peeled and grated ¼ cup chopped coriander leaves ¼ cup yogurt ¼ cup green chilli-ginger paste ¼ cup roasted and coarsely crushed peanuts Rock salt to taste
• 1 tablespoon ghee + ¼ cup ghee
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Add rajgira flour, potato, coriander, yogurt, green paste, rock salt and ghee in a bowl.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add water as required and form a lump-free batter.
4. Add 1 teaspoon ghee in each paniyaram mould and let it melt.
5. Once the ghee has melted, pour in the batter.
6. Cover and cook for 5-7 minutes.
7. Toss the paniyarams upside down in the moulds itself cover and cook for another 5-7 minutes.
8. Transfer to a serving plate, serve warm with coconut chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Comfort Flax Seed Laddoo
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Powerhouse of nutrition, you can enjoy these laddoos without feeling guilty

Cuisine: Indian Course: Mithais Prep: 21-25 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup Flax seeds
• 1 1/
• 2 cups Jaggery, chopped 1/
• 2 cup Raw peanuts 1/
• 2 cup Almonds 1/
• 2 cup Dried coconut, grated 1/
• 2 cup Oats 1/
• 2 cup White sesame seeds
• 2 tablespoons Black sesame seeds
• 2 tablespoons Garden cress (alive) seeds
• 1 teaspoon Green cardamom powder
• 1 tablespoon Ghee

Method
1. Warm a non-stick pan, add jaggery and ¼ cup water, cook, stirring, till a syrup of one string consistency is formed.
2. Warm another non-stick pan, add flax seeds and dry roast on medium heat for 1-2 minutes and set aside on a plate to cool down.
3. In the same pan dry roast raw peanuts on medium heat for 2-3 minutes, set aside with flax seeds.
4. Dry roast almonds, on medium low heat, for 2-3 minutes, keep along with flax seeds mixture.
5. Dry roast dried coconut and oats for 1-2 minutes and set aside along with the flax seed mixture, let it cool down completely.
6. Further dry roast white sesame seeds, black sesame seeds and aliv together for 1-2 minutes, set aside in a bowl.
7. Grind together the flax seed mixture to a coarse powder.
8. Spoon into a bowl, add the sesame seeds mixture and jaggery syrup and stir until combined Divide the mixture into equal portions and shape each portion into a laddoo and set aside on a plate to cool down to room temperature.
9. Once cooled completely store in an airtight container or serve.

Fresh Spring Roll Wrappers Mix
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Elevate your appetizers with our Fresh Spring Roll Wrappers Mix, perfect for crafting light and vibrant rolls bursting with crisp veggies and aromatic herbs.

Cuisine: Chinese Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 2-2.30 hour Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 2 tablespoons oil + for deep frying 1½ tablespoons finely chopped garlic
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger
• 1 spring onion, chopped with the greens
• 1 small carrot, cut into thin strips
• 0 grams shredded cabbage
• 1 cup mixed coloured capsicum strips
• 1 teaspoon vinegar
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take refined flour in a large bowl.
2. Add salt and 1¼ cups water little by little and mix with a wooden spoon till a smooth batter is formed.
3. Continue to mix for 5 minutes.
4. Cover and set aside to rest for 1½ hours.
5. To make the stuffing, heat oil in a non-stick wok.
6. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
7. Add garlic and ginger and fry gently for a minute.
8. Add spring onion bulb and fry gently for a few seconds.
9. Add carrot and cabbage and stir until combined.
10. Fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
11. Add coloured capsicum strips, vinegar, dark soy sauce, salt and crushed black peppercorns and stir until combined.
12. Add chicken and stir until combined.
13. Add spring onion greens and toss.
14. Remove the pan from the heat and allow the mixture to cool.Mix the rested batter well with your hand for 1-2 minutes.

16. Take a big portion of the dough and spread a thin layer of the batter in a circular motion and collect the excess dough and keep it aside.
17. Cook for 10-15 seconds, flip and cook for 1-2 seconds.
18. Remove from heat and set aside.
19. Similarly, make the remaining spring roll wrappers.
20. Use as required.
21. To make spring rolls, place a spring roll sheet on a worktop, place a generous portion of the stuffing in the centre.
22. Apply some refined flour slurry on the edges, fold the sides and tightly roll it.
23. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
24. Gently slide in the rolls a few at a time and deep fry till golden brown and crisp.
25. Drain on an absorbent paper.
26. Arrange them on a serving platter and serve warm with Schezwan sauce.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Fresh Tuvar Green Masala Delight
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is a winter recipe when fresh tuvar pods are available – absolutely flavourful. Fresh pigeon peas cooked with Indian spices and potatoes.

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Main Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 0 grams fresh tuvar pods, shelled 1/
• 2 cup fresh coriander-coconut-ginger-green chilli paste
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 1 medium onion, chopped
• 1 medium potato 1/
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm oil in an earthen pot, add onion and fry gently till golden brown.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Meanwhile slice potato and soak in a bowl of water.
4. Add turmeric powder to the onion mixture and stir until combined.
5. Add fresh coriander-coconut-ginger-green chilli paste, mix and fry gently well.
6. Drain and add potato and fresh tuvar and stir until combined.
7. Add salt and stir until combined.
8. Add 1 cup water, stir until combined, cover and cook until both potato and tuvar are cooked completely.
9. Spoon into a serving bowl, garnish with coriander sprig and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Fried Cheese Patty Stack
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Mozzarella cheese squares rolled in spiced breadcrumbs, deep-fried and served sandwiched in burger buns

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 2-2.30 hour Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 0 grams mozzarella cheese block, cut into
• 2 inch squares
• 4 mini burger buns
• 1 cup bread crumbs ½ teaspoon garlic power
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Spread bread crumbs on a plate.
2. Add garlic powder, onion powder, dried mixed herbs, salt and red chilli flakes and stir until combined.
3. Take refined flour in a bowl.
4. Add crushed black peppercorns, salt and pour ½ cup water and mix till well combined.
5. Dip the mozzarella squares into the refined flour mixture, coat with the bread crumbs mixture, and repeat the process one more time.
6. Place the prepared mozzarella squares into the freezer for 1-2 hours.
7. Slit the burger buns in half.
8. Warm butter in a non-stick grill pan, place the burger buns and toast for 30 seconds.
9. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
10. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai, gently slide in the frozen mozzarella squares and deep fry on medium heat till golden brown and crisp.
11. Drain on an absorbent paper.
12. To make the burger, apply some ketchup-mayo on the burger bun base and place some lettuce leaves.
13. Place the fried cheese and arrange tomato slice and onion rings.
14. Sprinkle crushed black pepper corns, salt and arrange some pickled jalapeno slices over it.
15. Arrange some more lettuce leaves on top.
16. Apply some ketchup-mayo on the base of the remaining burger half and place it on top to make a burger.
17. Secure it with a toothpick and garnish with black olives.
18. Serve at once.

Wholesome Gahat ka Shorba
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Made from nutrient-rich horse gram lentils simmered to perfection with aromatic spices, this hearty broth boasts a velvety texture and a depth of flavor that is truly unforgettable.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Soups Prep: 8-10 hour Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 2 cups horse gram (kulith), soaked overnight and boiled
• 1 tablespoon oil ¼ teaspoon asafoetida (hing)
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped garlic
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds (jeera)
• 1 teaspoon mustard seeds (rai)
• 1 tablespoon coriander seeds 2-
• 3 green chillies, finely chopped

Method
1. Warm oil in a non-stick pan.
2. Add asafoetida, garlic, ginger, cumin seeds, mustard seeds and coriander seeds and fry gently till fragrant.
3. Add green chillies and coriander stems and mix.
4. Add horse gram, crushed peppercorns and salt, stir until combined.
5. Add some water, cover and cook for 6-8 minutes.
6. Switch off heat, blend the gram mixture with a hand blender and strain.
7. Garnish with mint sprigs and serve warm.

Gajar Halwa Kulfi Bombs Edition
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Kulfis coated with gajar halwa and deep fried to a lovely golden crispiness

Cuisine: Indian Course: Mithais Prep: 1-1.30 hour Cook: 6-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 2 Tillewali malai kulfi to deep fry Oil
• 1 cup Refined flour (maida) 1/
• 2 cup Corn flour 1/
• 4 teaspoon Cinnamon powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon Vanilla essence
• 1 tablespo for garnish Powdered sugar a pinch Baking soda for drizzling Chocolate sauce for garnish Cocoa powder for garnish Chocolate vermicelli 10-
• 5 for garnish Walnuts

Method

2. Coat each piece of kulfi with gajar halwa and freeze them.

5. Add sufficient water and whisk well to make a thick and smooth batter.

8. Arrange them on a serving plate.
9. Drizzle chocolate sauce over them, sift cocoa powder and powdered sugar and sprinkle chocolate vermicelli on top.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Gluten Free Biscuit Khakra Treat
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

These are not only for the gluten intolerant people – they are so tasty that you are sure to enjoy them

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 teaspoon dried fenugreek leaves (kasuri methi) ½ teaspoon red chilli powder ¼ teaspoon cumin powder ¼ teaspoon coriander powder ½ tablespoon ginger-green chilli paste 1½ teaspoons sugar ¼ teaspoon dried mango powder (amchur) 2½ tablespoons ghee
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm ghee in a small pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Take amaranth flour in a large bowl, add barnyard millet flour, salt, dried fenugreek leaves, red chilli powder, cumin powder, coriander powder, ginger-green chilli paste, sugar and dried mango powder.
4. Add hot ghee to the flour mixture and stir until combined.
5. Add 1 cup water and knead into a soft dough.
6. Cover with a damp muslin cloth and set aside for 5 minutes.
7. Take a large portion of the dough and roll into a large thick disc.
8. Cut out roundels with a cookie cutter.
9. Warm a non-stick tawa.
10. Place a few discs on it and cook on medium heat for 2 minutes, flip and cook the other side for 2 minutes as well.
11. Press the discs with a wooden spoon so that the discs do not puff up.
12. Flip again and continue to press lightly.
13. Repeat the process till the discs turn golden and crisp on both sides.
14. Place them on a cooling wire rack for a few minutes or till they cool down to room temperature.
15. Arrange them on a serving platter and serve or store in an airtight container.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Wholesome Gobhi 65
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Spicy and crispy with just the right amount of sauce, this drool-worthy dish is an ideal starter!

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 41-50 minutes Cook: 15-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 large cauliflower (gobhi), cut into medium florets
• 1 tablespoon ginger-garlic paste
• 2 teaspoons red chilli powder ¾ teaspoon turmeric powder
• 3 teaspoons cumin powder
• 1 cup yogurt
• 3 tablespoons cornstarch
• 1 tablespoon refined flour (maida)
• 1 tablespoon oil + for deep-frying
• 1 teaspoon mustard seeds 3-
• 4 dried red chillies 6-
• 8 curry leaves 2-
• 4 green chillies, slit
• 1 inch ginger, finely chopped
• 1 tablespoon chopped garlic
• 1 tablespoon red chilli paste 2-
• 3 tablespoons sliced spring onion greens + for garnishing
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. To prepare the marinade, mix together ginger-garlic paste, 2 teaspoons chilli powder, ½ teaspoon turmeric powder, 2 teaspoons cumin powder, 2 teaspoons coriander powder, crushed peppercorns and 1 tablespoon lemon juice in a bowl.
2. Add salt and 2-3 tablespoons water and stir until combined.
3. Add cauliflower florets to the marinade and mix till well coated.
4. Set aside to marinate for 25-30 minutes.
5. Mix together yogurt, remaining chilli powder, remaining turmeric powder, cumin powder, coriander powder, salt and 2 tablespoons cornstarch alongwith 2-3 tablespoons water in another bowl.
6. Add remaining cornstarch and refined flour to the marinated cauliflower florets and stir until combined.
7. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
8. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
9. Put the marinated cauliflower florets and deep-fry till golden brown and crisp.
10. Drain on absorbent paper.
11. Warm 1 tablespoon oil in non-stick pan.
12. Add mustard seeds and let it splutter.
13. Add dried red chillies and fry gently for 30 seconds.
14. Add curry leaves and when it crackles, add green chillies, ginger and garlic and fry gently for 1 minute.
15. Add red chilli paste and fry gently for 1 minute.
16. Add yogurt mixture, mix and cook on low heat for 1-2 minutes.
17. Add 3-4 tablespoons water, mix and cook for 1-2 minutes.
18. Add salt and mix.
19. Add fried cauliflower florets, mix and cook for 1-2 minutes.
20. Add spring onion greens and mix.
21. Switch off heat, add remaining lemon juice and stir until combined.
22. Serve warm garnished with spring onion greens.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Gobhi Aloo Chaat Pakoda Treat
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

It is a flavourful Indian street food snack made from cauliflower (gobhi) and potato (aloo), mixed with spices, gram flour (besan), and herbs.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 large cauliflower (gobhi)
• 4 medium potatoes (aloo), boiled and peeled
• 1 tablespoon red chilli sauce ½ tablespoon tomato ketchup
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 2 teaspoons dried mango powder (amchur)
• 1 tablespoon chopped ginger 2-
• 3 green chillies, chopped
• 1 medium onion, finely chopped
• 1 tablespoon green chilli sauce
• 1 tablespoon green chutney + for topping Oil for deep-frying
• 1 tablespoon chopped fresh coriander leaves Grated radish (mooli) for topping Green capsicum strips for topping Red capsicum strips for topping Batter
• 1 cup gram flour
• 1 teaspoon carom seeds (ajwain) ¼ teaspoon turmeric powder ½ teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 tablespoon grated radish (mooli)
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped onion
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 1 teaspoon finely chopped ginger
• 2 green chillies, finely chopped
• 3 tablespoons green chilli sauce
• 2 tablespoons red chilli sauce
• 2 tablespoons tomato ketchup
• 2 tablespoons sugar Juice of ½ lemon
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take cauliflower florets in a bowl, add red chilli sauce, tomato ketchup, red chilli powder, 1 teaspoon dried mango powder, 1 teaspoon chaat masala and salt and stir until combined.
2. Mash potatoes in another bowl.
3. Add ginger, green chillies, onion, green chilli sauce, green chutney, remaining dried mango powder, remaining chaat masala and salt and stir until combined.
4. To make batter, take gram flour in a bowl, add carom seeds, turmeric powder, chilli powder, ginger-garlic paste, salt and water as required and whisk well to make a lump free batter of coating consistency.
5. Divide the potato mixture into equal portions, stuff each portion with a cauliflower floret and shape into balls.
6. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
7. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
8. To make sauce, heat oil in a non-stick pan, add ginger and green chillies and fry gently till fragrant.
9. Add green chilli sauce, red chilli sauce, tomato ketchup and sugar, mix and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
10. Spoon into a bowl.
11. Add lemon juice, radish, onion and coriander leaves and stir until combined.
12. Dip the balls in the batter and deep-fry in hot oil for a minute.
13. Drain on absorbent paper and flatten a little.
14. Deep-fry once again till golden brown.
15. Drain on absorbent paper.
16. Halve the pakodas and place them on a serving platter.
17. Serve them topped with green chutney, sauce, coriander leaves, radish, green and red capsicum strips.

If you are craving for a chilled frizzy yet unique drink, then here is the best recipe- Golden berry fizz. This is

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Beverages Cook: 0 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy

• 35-
• 0 cape gooseberries, halved ¾ cup pineapple juice ¼ cup lemon juice ½ cup sugar syrup Ice cubes as required Tonic water as required Mint sprig for garnish Cape gooseberries for garnish

Method

Spiced Green Chana Waffle
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Transforming traditional flavors, the green chana waffle offers a crispy, savory twist to your breakfast routine.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1½ cups fresh green chana, blanched 3-
• 4 garlic cloves 2-
• 3 green chillies
• 1 teaspoon baking powder
• 1 tablespoon olive oil + for greasing Microgreens for garnish Red radish slices to serve A few iceberg lettuce leaves to serve Pickled beetroot pieces to serve Garlic aioli to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Put green chana, garlic and broken green chillies into a food processer jar.
2. Roughly chop onion and add.
3. Add salt, cumin powder and a little water and process into a coarse paste.
4. Spoon into a large bowl.
5. Add gram flour and baking powder and stir until combined.
6. Add olive oil and stir until combined.
7. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
8. Warm a non-stick waffle iron, grease with oil and pour some mixture and spread it evenly.
9. Close and cook until golden and crisp.
10. Remove and place on a serving plate.
11. Make more waffles similarly.
12. Garnish with microgreens.
13. Serve warm with radish, lettuce, pickled beetroot and garlic aioli.

Comfort Gulab Jamun Kheer
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A great way to use up leftover mithais like gulab jamuns and kalakand – watch it getting devoured in no time

Cuisine: Indian Course: Mithais Prep: 2-2.30 hour Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 0 Mini gulab jamuns
• 1 litres Milk
• 1 teaspoon Green cardamom powder
• 3 tablespoons Mixed nuts, chopped
• 4 tablespoons Rice, soaked for
• 2 hours and coarsely ground
• 0 grams Kalakand, crumbled 1/
• 4 cup Jaggery, grated

Method
1. Boil milk in a non-stick pan.
2. Add cardamom powder and nuts and stir until combined.
3. Take some milk from the pan in a bowl, add rice, stir until combined and add back into the pan.
4. Mix and cook until the mixture thickens.
5. Add kalakand, mix and cook until well combined.
6. Add gulab jamun and stir until combined.
7. Add jaggery and mix till it melts.
8. Spoon into serving bowls and serve warm.

Quick Gulab Jamun Tartlets
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Tartlet shells filled with chocolate and gulab jamun mixture and served at room temperature

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Mithais Prep: 6-10 minutes Cook: 0-5 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 5 Mini gulab jamuns
• 4 Tartlet shells 1/
• 2 cup Cream
• 3 Caramel chocolate bars 10-
• 5 Walnuts to garnish Rabdi to garnish Pistachios, chopped

Method
1. Warm cream in a non-stick pan, stirring continuously with a spatula.
2. Spoon into a bowl.
3. Break caramel chocolate bars and put into the same bowl, keep the bowl in a Microwave and heat for 1 minute.
4. Take the bowl out and stir until combined till the chocolate melts completely and becomes smooth.
5. Chop walnuts, add to the melted chocolate and stir until combined.
6. Add mini gulab jamuns and stir until combined.
7. Place the tartlet shells on the worktop, fill them up with this chocolate mixture.
8. Garnish with rabdi and pistachios.
9. Arrange the tartlets on a serving platter and serve at once.

Hare Pyaaz , Neem aur Imli ki Relish
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A very unique combination of spring onions, neem leaves and tamarind made into a chutney. Best enjoyed with missi roti.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Pickles, Jams and Chutneys Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 6 stalks spring onion greens 15-
• 0 neem leaves Marble siz tamarind, soaked in warm water
• 1 tablespoon oil
• 1 1/
• 2 tablespoons garlic, chopped
• 1 tablespoon ginger, finely chopped
• 1 tablespoon white sesame seeds (safed til) 6-
• 7 green chillies, chopped 1/
• 1 teaspoon black salt
• 1 teaspoon roasted cumin powder Missi roti to serve

Method
1. Warm oil in a non-stick pan, add garlic, ginger and white sesame seeds.
2. Fry gently for a minute on medium heat, stirring continuously.
3. Add green chillies and fry gently for a minute.
4. Add asafoetida and stir until combined.
5. Add neem leaves and fry gently for a minute.
6. Roughly chop spring onions greens and add to the pan.
7. Squeeze tamarind pulp and add to the pan and cook for 2-3 minutes.
8. Add salt and stir until combined.
9. Cook for 1-2 minutes on medium heat.
10. Add black salt and roasted cumin powder and stir until combined.
11. Cook for 1-2 minutes and switch off heat.
12. Let the mixture cool slightly.
13. Transfer the mixture into a blender jar and grind into fine paste.
14. Spoon into a small serving bowl and set aside to cool.
15. Serve with missi roti.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Creamy Hercules Bar
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A powerhouse of flavor with a mighty combination of nuts, caramel, and chocolate, guaranteed to satisfy your cravings and fuel your day.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 41-50 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup walnuts
• 3 tablespoons ghee + for greasing
• 1 cup finger millet (nachni) flour ½ cup roasted peanuts
• 1 cup Indian gooseberry (amla) candies 1½ cups chopped jaggery ½ teaspoon green cardamom powder
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Grease a tray with ghee.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Blend walnuts to a coarse powder.
4. Warm ghee in a deep stainless steel pan, add nachni flour and California walnut powder, mix and fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
5. Blend peanuts to a fine powder and add to the pan.
6. Mix and cook on low heat for 2-3 minutes, stirring continuously.
7. Chop amla candies and add to the pan.
8. Mix and cook, stirring continuously, on low heat for 4-5 minutes or till the colour of mixture changes to brown.
9. Add jaggery and green cardamom powder and mix.
10. Cook until jaggery melts and mixture leaves the sides of the pan.
11. Pour the mixture into the greased tray and spread evenly.
12. Set aside to cool down.
13. Cut into bars and serve at once.

Homemade Caramel Frozen Cream
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Your kids will lap up this ice cream in no time – it is so very flavourful

• 2 cup + for drizzling Caramel syrup
• 1 1/
• 2 cups Milk 1/
• 2 cup Milk powder
• 2 tablespoons Sugar
• 1 cup Fresh cream

Method
1. Warm milk and milk powder in a non-stick pan.
2. Cook, stirring, till the mixture thickens.

Kitchen Instant Panjeeri
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Popular Punjabi sweet made with arrowroot biscuits instead of whole wheat flour

Cuisine: Punjabi Course: Mithais Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 4 cups Arrowroot biscuits, crushed 1/
• 2 cup Powdered sugar 4-
• 5 tablespoons Ghee
• 1 tablespoon Edible gum 8-
• 0 Almonds, slivered 8-
• 0 Raisins 8-
• 0 Pistachios, slivered
• 1 pinch Salt

Method
1. Blend the biscuits to a coarse powder and transfer into a bowl.
2. Blend sugar to a fine powder and transfer into another bowl.
3. Warm ghee in a deep non-stick pan, add gond and fry till puffed and crisp.
4. Drain into a third bowl.
5. Add almonds and raisins to the same pan.
6. When raisins become puff up, add pistachios and biscuit powder and stir until combined.
7. Add fried gond and stir until combined and switch off heat.
8. Crush the gond.
9. Spoon into a bowl and lightly crush the mixture.
10. Add powdered sugar.
11. Again crush the mixture.
12. Add a pinch of salt and stir until combined.
13. Spoon into a serving bowl and serve at once.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Fresh Instant Rice Dhokla
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Do you have some rice left at home? Here is an amazing way to give your leftover rice a new life. This instant dhokla as the name suggests can be made instantly and tastes flavourful.

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 15-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 1 cup leftover cooked rice ¾ cup yogurt
• 1 cup semolina (rawa)
• 2 teaspoons ginger-green chilli paste 1½ tablespoons finely chopped green capsicum 1½ tablespoons finely chopped yellow capsicum 1½ tablespoons finely chopped red capsicum 1½ tablespoons finely chopped carrot 1½ tablespoons finely chopped spring onion bulbs
• 1 teaspoon sugar Oil for greasing
• 1 teaspoon fruit salt Tempering
• 1 tablespoon oil
• 1 teaspoon mustard seeds A pinch of asafoetida (hing) 10-
• 2 curry leaves 1½ teaspoons white sesame seeds

Method
1. Put cooked rice in a blender jar.
2. Add yogurt and blend to a fine paste.
3. Transfer this into a large bowl.
4. Add ginger-green chilli paste, green capsicum, yellow capsicum, red capsicum, carrot, spring onion bulbs, spring onion greens, salt, sugar and 1 cup water and mix till well combined.
5. Warm sufficient water in a steamer.
6. Grease a dhokla thali with some oil.
7. Add fruit salt into the batter and stir until combined.
8. Transfer this into the greased thali and place it in the steamer and steam for 10-15 minutes or till done.
9. Switch the heat off and allow to cool.
10. Cut the dhokla into squares.
11. To make the tempering, heat oil in a small pan.
12. Add mustard seeds and let them splutter.
13. Add asafoetida, curry leaves and white sesame seeds and fry gently for a few seconds.
14. Pour this immediately over the dhokla.
15. Arrange on a serving plate and serve with green chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Quick Jackfruit Seed Sambhar
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A unique sambhar loaded with flavours that will leave you asking for more

Cuisine: South Indian Course: Dals and Kadhis Prep: 41-50 minutes Cook: 41-50 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 4 teaspoon Turmeric powder Sambhar
• 1 tablespoon Oil 1/
• 4 teaspoon Fenugreek seeds (methi dana) 15-
• 0 Madras onions, peeled 20-
• 5 Curry leaves 1/
• 2 teaspoon Turmeric powder
• 1 1/
• 2 tablespoons Sambhar masala powder 3/
• 4 cup Tamarind water, (lemon sized tamarind ball soaked in 3/
• 4 cup war 1/
• 2 cup Split pigeon peas (tuvar dal), soaked for
• 0 minutes, boiled with
• 1 cup water,
• 1 tablespoon Jaggery, chopped 1/
• 4 teaspoon Asafoetida
• 2 tablespoons Fresh coriander leaves, chopped Tempering
• 1 tablespoon Ghee
• 1 teaspoon Mustard seeds 10-
• 2 Curry leaves To serve as required Steamed rice for drizzling Ghee as required Pappadums, fried

Method
1. Remove the seeds from each jackfruit kernel and wash them well.
2. Put the seeds in a pressure cooker, add 1½ cups water, salt and turmeric powder, mix, and cook for 3-4 whistles.
3. Open the cooker once the pressure has reduced completely.
4. Drain the seeds and let them cool slightly.
5. Peel them and keep them in the strainer.
6. To make the sambhar, heat oil in a non-stick deep pan, add fenugreek seeds and fry gently for a few seconds.
7. Add madras onions and 10-12 curry leaves, mix and fry gently till onions turn golden brown.
8. Add the peeled jackfruit seeds, mix and fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
9. Add turmeric powder and sambhar powder, mix and fry gently for 30 seconds Add tamarind water and 1 cup water and stir until combined.
10. Add salt and stir until combined.
11. Bring it to a boil.
12. Continue to cook on medium heat for 15-20 minutes.
13. Add boiled pigeon peas and stir until combined.
14. Add 1 cup water, jaggery, asafoetida and remaining curry leaves and stir until combined.
15. Bring the mixture to a boil.
16. Add coriander leaves and stir until combined.
17. To make the tempering, heat ghee in a small non-stick pan, add mustard seeds and let them splutter.
18. Add curry leaves and fry gently for a few seconds.
19. Immediately pour this into the prepared sambhar and stir until combined.
20. Transfer the sambhar into a serving bowl, garnish with coriander leaves and serve warm with steamed rice drizzled with ghee and fried papadams.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Classic Jaggery Cake
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Whole wheat flour and jaggery made this cake healthy

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Desserts Prep: 41-50 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 4 cup Jaggery powder 1/
• 2 cup Yogurt 1/
• 4 cup Oil
• 1 teaspoon Vanilla essence
• 1 cup Whole wheat flour (gehun ka atta)
• 1 teaspoon Baking powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Baking soda a pinch Cinnamon powder 1/
• 4 cup Milk

Method
1. Preheat an oven to 180° C Take the yogurt in a bowl, add oil, vanilla essence and jaggery powder and beat with an electric beater till the jaggery melts.
2. Sift the whole wheat flour, baking powder, baking soda and cinnamon powder directly into the yogurt mixture and mix and fold in.
3. Add milk and mix gently till a smooth batter is formed.
4. Pour the batter into a 6 x 2 x 2 inch silicon rectangular cake mould and tap a few times.
5. Place the cake mould on a baking tray, keep the tray in the preheated oven and bake for 25-30 minutes.
6. Bring the baking tray out of the oven and allow the cake to cool completely.
7. De-mould and slice.
8. Arrange the slices on a serving platter and serve.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Jain Corn Tamatar Nu Shaak Mix
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Spicy, creamy and yet superbly Jain, even without the onion and garlic. Serve hot with parathas. This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Course- Veg Cook: 15-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy

Ingredients
• 1 corn on the cob
• 4 medium tomatoes, roughly chopped Gravy 2-
• 3 tablespoons oil
• 1 inch cinnamon stick 4-
• 5 cloves 4-
• 5 black peppercorns
• 1 dried red chilli
• 1 tablespoon melon seeds
• 2 medium tomatoes, finely chopped
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds ¼ teaspoon asafoetida (hing)
• 1 medium green capsicum, finely chopped 2-
• 3 green chillies, chopped
• 1 teaspoon coriander powder ½ teaspoon garam masala powder
• 1 teaspoon sugar
• 2 tablespoons fresh cream ¼ teaspoon green cardamom powder
• 1 teaspoon dried fenugreek leaves (kasuri methi)
• 1 teaspoon lemon juice 1½ tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves Fresh coriander sprig for garnish Paranthe to serve

Method
1. Roast the corn on the cob on direct flame till well charred from all sides.
2. Place the corn on the worktop and remove the corn kernels with the help of a knife.
3. Put the corn kernels into a blender jar.
4. Blend to a coarse paste.
5. Warm oil in a non-stick pan.
6. Add cinnamon stick, cloves, black peppercorns, and dried red chilli and fry gently for a few seconds.
7. Add tomatoes and stir until combined.
8. Cook until soft and pulpy.
9. Add melon seeds and roasted peanuts and stir until combined.
10. Cook for 1-2 minutes.
11. Add turmeric powder and salt and stir until combined.
12. Add ½ cup water, mix and cook for 2-3 minutes.
13. Switch the heat off, discard the cinnamon stick and allow the mixture to cool slightly.
14. Put the mixture into a blender jar and blend to a fine paste.
15. For the tempering, heat oil in a non-stick pan.
16. Add cumin seeds and asafoetida and stir until combined.
17. Add green capsicum and mix, cook for 1-2 minutes.
18. Add green chillies, and tomatoes and stir until combined.
19. Cook for 1-2 minutes.
20. Add blended paste, mix and cook for 1 minute.
21. Add blended corn mixture and stir until combined.
22. Add 1 cup water and mix.
23. Add coriander powder, garam masala powder, cumin powder, chaat masala, and red chilli powder and stir until combined.
24. Cook for 2-3 minutes.
25. Add cottage cheese and mix.
26. Add salt, sugar and fresh cream and stir until combined.
27. Add green cardamom powder, dried fenugreek leaves and lemon juice and stir until combined.
28. Add coriander leaves, mix and switch the heat off.
29. Transfer the mixture into a serving bowl.
30. Garnish with coriander sprig.
31. Serve warm with paranthe.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Jain Mix Vegetable Makhani Favourite
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

With coconut cream,semi-ripe bananas and assorted vegetables, this makhani recipe gets a Jain twist. This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy

Ingredients
• 10-
• 2 small cauliflower florets, boiled and drained 6-
• 8 French beans, cut into
• 1 inch pieces, blanched and drained ¼ cup boiled green peas
• 1 medium raw banana, boiled, peeled and cut into
• 1 inch cubes
• 2 tablespoons oil 5-
• 6 black peppercorns
• 1 inch cinnamon stick 2-
• 3 cloves
• 3 green cardamoms
• 1 tablespoon red chilli paste
• 1 teaspoon sugar
• 1 tablespoon butter ¼ teaspoon dried fenugreek leaves powder Fresh coriander leaves for garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm oil in a non-stick pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add black peppercorns, cinnamon stick, cloves, green cardamoms, and stir until combined.
4. Cook until fragrant.
5. Add red chilli paste, mix and cook for 1-2 minutes.
6. Add tomato puree, mix and cook until the oil separates.
7. Add salt, turmeric powder and stir until combined.
8. Cook for 1-2 minutes.
9. Stir in the coconut cream and cook for 1-2 minutes.
10. Add sugar and mix, cook until it melts.
11. Add cauliflower, French beans, green peas, and raw bananas and stir until combined.
12. Cook for 2-3 minutes.
13. Add butter and stir until combined.
14. Cook for 1-2 minutes.
15. Add dried fenugreek leaves powder and stir until combined.
16. Spoon into a serving bowl.
17. Garnish with fresh coriander leaves.
18. Serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Everyday Jain Pakoda
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Simple and flavourful pakodas made with cabbage and gram flour. Simply moreish ! In this Jain version, onions are replaced with cabbage . This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 1 small cabbage, thinly shredded
• 1 cup gram flour (besan)
• 2 tablespoons rice flour
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 1 teaspoon sugar ¼ teaspoon asafoetida (hing)
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder ½ teaspoon crushed black peppercorns
• 2 teaspoons oil + for deep frying Jain green chutney to serve

Method
1. Take cabbage in a large bowl.
2. Add gram flour, rice flour, coriander leaves, sugar, asafoetida, red chilli powder, crushed black peppercorns, lemon juice, salt, and oil and stir until combined.
3. Add ½ cup water and mix till a thick mixture is formed.
4. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
5. Gently slide in small portions of the mixture and deep fry till golden brown and crisp.
6. Drain on an absorbent paper.
7. Arrange the pakodas on a serving plate and serve warm with Jain green chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Everyday Jain Seekh Kebab
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Finger food gets a Jain twist. The joy of a pure veg seekh kebab with veggies and raisins that are very succulent. This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 4 medium raw bananas, boiled, peeled and mashed
• 4 tablespoons oil 2-
• 3 green chillies, finely chopped ½ cup finely chopped cabbage ¼ teaspoon turmeric powder
• 5 raisins (kismis)
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 1 tablespoons finely chopped fresh mint leaves
• 2 tablespoons roasted gram flour Cabbage salad to serve Jain green chutney to serve Fresh mint leaves for garnish Lemon wedges to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm 1 tbsp oil in a non-stick pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add green chillies and cabbage and stir until combined.
4. Add turmeric powder and stir until combined.
5. Cook for 1 minute.
6. Remove the pan from the heat and allow to cool slightly.
7. Take boiled raw banana in a large bowl, add the cabbage mixture, red chilli powder, chaat masala, salt, green cardamom powder, raisins, coriander leaves, mint leaves, and roasted gram flour and mix till well combined.
8. Dip your fingers in some water and take a portion of the mixture and spread it on to a satay stick and shape it like a seekh.
9. Warm remaining oil in a non-stick pan.
10. Place the seekh on it and cook for 3-4 minutes, rotating the seekh in between till golden brown on all sides.
11. Arrange the seekhs on a serving plate, garnish with mint leaves and serve warm with salad, Jain green chutney, and lemon wedges.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Quick Jeera Kulcha Biscuits
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

The satisfying fusion of Indian spices and buttery goodness in every crunchy nibble.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds Caraway seeds (shahi jeera) to sprinkle
• 0 grams salted butter, softened
• 0 grams powdered sugar
• 1 teaspoon vanilla essence
• 0 grams refined flour (maida)
• 0 grams rice flour
• 2 tablespoons chopped mixed nuts (almonds and pistachios) + to sprinkle White sesame seeds (safed til) to sprinkle Sea salt to sprinkle
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Preheat oven to 180º C.
2. Take butter and powdered sugar in a bowl and cream with a wooden spoon till the mixture becomes light in colour.
3. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
4. Add vanilla essence and cumin seeds and stir until combined.
5. Sift together refined flour, rice flour into the butter mixture.
6. Add mixed nuts and stir until combined.
7. Sprinkle little water and make medium soft dough.
8. Divide the dough into equal portions and shape into thick discs.
9. Grease a baking tray with oil.
10. Place the discs on it.
11. Sprinkle caraway seeds, white sesame seeds and sea salt and press lightly.
12. Sprinkle chopped mixed nuts and press lightly.
13. Place the tray in the preheated oven and bake for 15-20 minutes.
14. Allow to cool.
15. Arrange on a serving platter and serve.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Homestyle Jowar Pumpkin Gnocchi
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A wholesome twist on classic gnocchi, this dish blends sweet potato, pumpkin, and sorghum flour, tossed in a fragrant garlic-herb pumpkin sauce and topped with parmesan.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Noodles and Pastas Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 1/
• 2 cups sorghum flour (jowar ka atta)
• 2 cups boiled pumpkin puree
• 3 tablespoons olive oil
• 2 tablespoons finely chopped garlic 1/
• 2 tablespoon chopped fresh mixed herbs 8-
• 0 fresh basil leaves + for garnish
• 1 teaspoon red chilli flakes
• 0 grams parmesan cheese
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take sorghum flour in a mixing bowl, add sweet potatoes and mash with a fork.
2. Add 1 cup pumpkin puree, salt and crushed black peppercorns, and knead into a dough.
3. To make gnocchi, take small portions of the dough and shape them into cylinders.
4. Flatten the edges.
5. Warm sufficient water in a deep non-stick deep pan and let it come to a boil.
6. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
7. Drop in the gnocchis into the boiling water and cook until they float on the surface.
8. Meanwhile to make the sauce, heat oil in a shallow non-stick pan, add garlic and fry gently well.
9. Add the remaining pumpkin puree and stir until combined.
10. Strain the cooked gnocchi and add to the sauce and toss gently till they are well coated with the sauce.
11. Add salt and mixed herbs.
12. Tear basil leaves and add to the gnocchi.
13. Add red chilli flakes and gently toss on high heat.
14. Spoon into a serving plate, grate parmesan cheese on top and garnish with basil leaves.
15. Serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Comfort Kadai Kala Chana
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Freshly ground kadai masala makes this kala chana dish extra special. Brown Bengal gram cooked with Indian spices.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Prep: 7-8 hour Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 2 cups brown Bengal gram (kala chana), soaked overnight and boiled
• 1 tablespoon coriander seeds
• 1 teaspoon fennel seeds
• 4 dried red chillies
• 2 teaspoons cumin seeds
• 3 tablespoons mustard oil
• 2 bay leaves
• 2 green chillies, finely chopped 1/
• 2 cup tomato puree
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 1/
• 2 teaspoons rried mango powder (amchur) 1/
• 2 teaspoon turmeric powder
• 2 tablespoons coriander powder
• 2 medium tomatoes
• 1 inch ginger, cut into thin strips + to garnish 8-
• 0 fresh coriander sprigs to garnish 1/
• 2 tablespoon lemon juice
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. To make the kadai masala, dry roast coriander seeds, fennel seeds, 2 dried chillies and 1 tsp cumin seeds till fragrant.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Remove from heat, cool and grind to a coarse powder.
4. Warm mustard oil in a non-stick kadai till it begins to smoke.
5. Cool it slightly, add bay leaves and remaining dried chillies and fry gently for 10 seconds.
6. Add green chillies and remaining cumin seeds, mix and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
7. Add tomato puree, stir until combined and cook for 2 minutes.
8. Add red chilli powder, dried mango powder, turmeric powder, coriander powder and chaat masala and stir until combined.
9. Add salt and mix.
10. Roughly chop tomatoes, add, mix and cook until they turn soft and pulpy.
11. Add ginger strips and stir until combined.
12. Add brown Bengal gram and mix.
13. Add water as required, mix and cook for 5-6 minutes.
14. Chop some coriander sprigs, add and mix.
15. Add the kadai masala and lemon juice and stir until combined.
16. Spoon into a serving bowl, garnish with ginger strips and coriander sprigs and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Kaddu ki Khatti Meethi Subzi Edition
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Red pumpkin is a vegetable not many like to eat, but when cooked this way they will not be able to resist

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Prep: 10-15 minutes Cook: 15-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet & Sour Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 0 grams red pumpkin (kaddu), peeled and cut into
• 1 inch cubes
• 3 tablespoons tamarind paste 1/
• 2 cup grated jaggery
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger, 1-
• 2 green chillies, finely chopped 1/
• 4 teaspoon asafoetida 1/
• 4 teaspoon turmeric powder
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 tablespoon chopped fresh coriander leaves,
• 1 green chilli, slit Fresh coriander sprig for garnish

Method
1. Warm oil in a non-stick pan, add ginger and green chillies, mix and fry gently till fragrant.
2. Add asafoetida, mix and fry gently for a minute.
3. Add pumpkin cubes and cook for 4-5 minutes.
4. Add turmeric powder, red chilli powder, 1 tsp garam masala powder and salt, stir until combined and fry gently for a few minutes.
5. Add tamarind paste and jaggery, mix and cook until the jaggery melts.
6. Add some water, mix, cover and cook for 8-10 minutes.
7. Mash the pumpkins slightly, add coriander leaves and remaining garam masala powder and stir until combined.
8. Spoon into a serving bowl, garnish with slit green chilli and coriander sprig and serve warm.

Wholesome Kalakand Fruit Custard
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

If you have lots of leftover kalakand, don’t despair. Just follow this recipe and turn out a unique dessert

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Desserts Prep: 2-2.30 hour Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 5 pieces Kalakand
• 1 Red apple, cored, cut into 1/
• 2 inch pieces and soaked in wa
• 1 Pear, cored and cut into 1/
• 2 inch pieces
• 2 1/
• 2 tablespoons Custard powder 15-
• 0 Walnuts
• 1 1/
• 2 cups Milk 4-
• 5 tablespoons Caster sugar to garnish Orange segments to garnish Fresh pomegranate pears to garnish Red grapes, seeded and halved

Method
1. Roughly chop walnuts.
2. Dry roast the walnuts in a non-stick pan till crisp.
3. Spoon into a bowl.
4. Take milk in another bowl, add custard powder and stir until combined.
5. Transfer the custard mixture into a deep non-stick pan and cook on medium heat, whisking continuously till the mixture starts thickening and reaches pouring consistency.
6. Add castor sugar.
7. Break kalakand pieces and add and stir until combined with a whisk till the mixture thickens further.
8. Add half the roasted walnuts and apple and stir until combined.
9. Pour half the custard into a deep serving plate.
10. Add pears and pour the remaining custard mixture.
11. Garnish with orange segments, pomegranate pearls, remaining roasted walnuts and red grapes.
12. Refrigerate for 1-2 hours or till set.
13. Serve chilled.
