Kale Chane ki Kadhi Twist
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Kale chane simmered in a flavoured curd gravy and served with steamed rice is an absolute soul food. This is

Cuisine: Rajasthani Course: Dals and Kadhis Cook: 20-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup boiled brown chickpeas (kala chana) 1½ cups yogurt
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 2 teaspoons coriander powder
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder
• 2 green chillies, chopped
• 2 tablespoons ghee
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds ¼ teaspoon asafoetida (hing)
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves Red chilli oil for drizzling Steamed rice to serve

Method
1. Take yogurt in a large bowl, add gram flour, turmeric powder, salt, red chilli powder, coriander powder, garam masala powder and green chillies and whisk till a lump free mixture is formed.
2. Add 3 cups water and stir until combined.
3. Warm ghee in a deep pan.
4. Add cumin seeds and let them change colour.
5. Add asafoetida and cook for a few seconds.
6. Add boiled brown chickpeas and stir until combined.
7. Add the yogurt mixture and stir until combined.
8. Cook for 10-15 minutes or till done.
9. Add coriander leaves, mix and cook for 1 minute.
10. Transfer the kadhi into a serving bowl and serve warm with steamed rice.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Kale Chane with Rajgira Puri Treat
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Whole black gram soaked and cooked with simple spices. This flavourful bowl is best had with rajgira puris. This recipe featured on Food Food Channel.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Course- Vegetarain Cook: 20-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 1½ cups black Bengal gram (kale chane), soaked overnight, drained and boiled in fresh water
• 1 cup amaranth (rajgira) flour
• 1 tablespoon +
• 1 teaspoon oil + for deep-frying
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm 1 tbsp oil in a non-stick pan, add cumin seeds and once the seeds change colour, add ginger and fry gently for ½ a minute.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add coriander powder and stir until combined.
4. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
5. Add black gram, black salt, red chilli powder and black Bengal gram stock to the pan and stir until combined.
6. Cook until the black gram is done.
7. Put amaranth flour into a bowl, grate potato into it.
8. Add salt and stir until combined.
9. Add ½ cup water and knead into a soft dough.
10. Add 1 tsp oil and knead again.
11. Divide the dough into equal portions, shape them into balls and roll out into small pooris.
12. Deep-fry pooris in hot oil till brown and crisp on both the sides.
13. Drain on absorbent paper.
14. Transfer the chane into a serving bowl, place it on one side of the serving plate, place fried pooris on the other side and serve at once.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Creamy Kapnya
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Traditional Maharashtrian snack – ideally should be served with cups of hot tea

Cuisine: Maharashtrian Course: Mithais Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup Whole wheat flour (gehun ka atta) 1/
• 4 cup Jaggery, chopped 1/
• 4 cup Gram flour (besan) a pinch Salt 1/
• 2 tablespoon Fennel sees (saunf) 1/
• 2 tbsp + to deep fry Hot oil to sprinkle Poppy seeds

Method
1. Warm ¼ cup water in a deep non-stick pan, add jaggery and cook until it melts.
2. Take whole wheat flour in a bowl, add gram flour, salt, fennel seeds and hot oil and stir until combined.
3. Add the jaggery mixture and knead into stiff dough.
4. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
5. Roll out the dough into a thin disk and sprinkle poppy seeds on top.
6. Press lightly.
7. Cut into diamonds, slide them into hot oil and deep fry, on medium heat, till crisp.
8. Drain on absorbent paper.
9. Allow to cool and serve or store in airtight containers.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Comfort Karachi Halwa
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Chewy rich halwa. This recipe has featured on the show Khanakhazana.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Mithais Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Difficult

Ingredients
• 1 1/
• 4 cup Cornflour Sugar
• 2 cups Sugar
• 1 teaspoon Lemon juice 1-
• 2 drops Orange essence
• 0 grams Ghee a few drops Rose water 10-
• 2 Almonds finely chopped 10-
• 2 Pistachios finely chopped

Method
1. Dissolve cornflour in three fourth cup of water.
2. Grease a tray.
3. Mix sugar with one cup of water and bring it to a boil.
4. Add lemon juice, bring it to a boil again and then remove scum.
5. Add edible orange colour and mix.
6. Reduce heat to medium, add a little cornflour mixture and stir continuously.
7. When it boils add a little cornflour mixture again.
8. Continue the same process until all the cornflour mixture is used up.
9. Cook until mixture is thickened and starts sticking.
10. Reduce heat to low.
11. Add a little melted ghee to it and stir until combined.
12. When ghee is absorbed, add a little more and let it absorb.
13. Continue till all the ghee is used up.
14. Take one teaspoon of mixture onto a plate, cool it and if it does not stick to the plate, remove pan from heat.
15. Add rose water, stir until combined and then spread it on a greased tray.
16. Sprinkle chopped almonds and pistachios on top.
17. Cut into desired size and shape.
18. Serve when cool.
19. Nutrition Info Calories 3434 Carbohydrates 576.8 Protein 21.9 Fat 115.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Simple Kayloli
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Dosa with a twist! Enjoy a tantalizing snack with spiced batter, promising a deliciously unique flavor experience.

Cuisine: Karnataka Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1½ cups rice flour ½ cup semolina (rawa)
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take rice flour in a bowl, add semolina, green chillies, red chilli powder, turmeric powder, salt and stir until combined.
2. Add milk, 1½ cups water and stir until combined.
3. Set aside for 10-15 minutes.
4. Warm a non-stick tawa.
5. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
6. Spread a ladleful of batter evenly.
7. Drizzle some oil around and cook on medium heat for 2-3 minutes or till the underside is golden brown.
8. Flip and cook on other side for 2-3 minutes more.
9. Serve warm with coconut chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Wholesome Kharzi
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Check out this cheesy and easy Rice recipe kharzi from Arunachal Pradesh. Mozzarella cheese, chillies and ginger are blended to make a fine paste. Then cooked rice is tossed in this paste along with...

Cuisine: Arunachal Pradesh Course: Rice Cook: 8-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy

Ingredients
• 1½ cups grated mozzarella cheese
• 5 dried red chillies, stemmed and soaked
• 1 inch ginger, chopped
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 3 cups cooked rice
• 2 tablespoons chopped spring onion greens + for garnish
• 1 teaspoon red chilli flakes
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Transfer mozzarella cheese in a blender jar.
2. Drain dried red chillies and add along with ginger.
3. Add ¾ cup water and grind to a fine paste.
4. Warm oil in a nonstick pan.
5. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
6. Add spring onion bulbs and fry gently for a minute.
7. Add the prepared paste and fry gently for 3-4 minutes.
8. Add salt, crushed black peppercorns and stir until combined.
9. Add rice and stir until combined.
10. Cook for 2-3 minutes.
11. Add spring onion greens, red chilli flakes and stir until combined.
12. Serve warm garnished with spring onion greens.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Kitchen Kurmura Energy Bars
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

An excellent snack to be had when hunger pangs strike in between meals – full of nutrients it is very healthy.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 21-25 minutes Cook: 6-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 3 cups puffed rice (kurmura) ¾ cup chopped jaggery
• 2 tablespoons ghee + for greasing ¼ cup creamy peanut butter
• 1 tablespoon mixed fruit jam ¼ cup crushed roasted peanuts
• 1 cup melted dark chocolate Salt for garnishing Walnuts for garnishing Roasted and peeled peanuts for garnishing Roasted Bengal gram for garnishing Fresh coriander sprigs for garnishing
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm a non-stick wok, add jaggery, ghee and peanut butter and cook until jaggery melts.Add jam and stir until combined, add puffed rice, crushed peanuts and stir until combined.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Grease a steel tray with ghee.
4. Transfer the mixture onto the tray and spread it evenly, roll a rolling pin over it to level the top and set aside until it sets.Demould and cut into thick sticks and then cut each stick into half.Dip half the bar pieces in melted dark chocolate and place it on a wire rack, sprinkle some salt on it.
5. Garnish the rest with walnuts, peanuts, roasted Bengal gram and coriander sprigs.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Everyday Kuttu ka Parantha
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A healthy and wholesome buckwheat flour paratha. Kuttu ka paratha is a flatbread that can be eaten with some condiment or as it is alone. This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 8-10 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 1 cup buckwheat flour (kuttu)
• 1 tablespoon ginger-green chilli paste Ghee as required
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Grate potatoes in a bowl.
2. Add chopped coriander, peanuts, rock salt, ginger-green chilli paste and buckwheat flour and stir until combined.
3. Add some water and knead into a stiff dough.
4. Cover with a damp muslin cloth and set aside for 15 minutes.
5. Divide the dough into equal portions and shape them into balls.
6. Put some ghee on a non-stick tawa.
7. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
8. Place a ball over, dip palms in water and spread the ball to make a thin disc.
9. Switch on the heat, spread some ghee, cover and cook from both sides on medium heat till golden and crisp.
10. Serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Lachchedar Aloo Tikki Chaat Edition
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A very popular chaat in North India – potato tikkis served with yogurt and other chaat ingredients.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 4 large potatoes, boiled and peeled
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger
• 1 teaspoon chaat masala + to sprinkle
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder + to sprinkle
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds ½ teaspoon carom seeds (ajwain)
• 2 tablespoons raisins Ghee for shallow frying 1½ cups thick yogurt, beaten 1½ tablespoons powdered sugar
• 1 teaspoon rose water Tamarind chutney as required Green chutney as required Nylon sev as required Pomegranate pearls as required Fresh mint sprig for garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Grate potatoes into a bowl.
2. Add ginger, green chillies, salt, chaat masala, red chilli powder, cumin seeds, carom seeds and raisins and mix the mixture lightly.
3. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
4. Divide the mixture equal portions and shape them into tikkis.
5. Warm ghee in a pan, add the tikkis and shallow fry, turning sides, on medium heat till evenly golden brown on both sides.
6. They should be soft from inside and crisp on the outside.
7. Take yogurt in a bowl, add powdered sugar and rose water and stir until combined.Drain tikkis on absorbent paper.
8. Spread a spoon of yogurt mixture on a serving platter with back of a spoon.
9. Place tikkis over it, drizzle yogurt, a spoon of tamarind chutney and few drops of green chutney over the tikkis.
10. Sprinkle sev and add pomegranate pearls on top.
11. Sprinkle chaat masala and red chilli powder.
12. Garnish with a mint sprig and serve at once.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Spiced Lachchedar Mathri
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Crispy layers of indulgence, boasting a flaky texture and a tantalizing blend of spices for a snack that's both crunchy and addictive.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 2 cups refined flour (maida) + for dusting
• 1 teaspoon carom seeds (ajwain)
• 6 tablespoons ghee + as required Green Thai curry paste as required Red Thai curry paste as required Oil for greasing and brushing
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Preheat oven to 180˚ C.
2. Mix together refined flour, carom seeds, crushed peppercorns, salt and 6 tablespoons ghee in a bowl.
3. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
4. Add water as required and knead into a stiff dough.
5. Divide the dough into two equal portions and roll out each into a thin sheet.
6. Spread some green Thai curry paste on one sheet, top with some ghee, dust some flour and roll into a cylinder.
7. Spread some red Thai curry paste on the other sheet, top with some ghee, dust some flour and roll into a cylinder.
8. Cut both cylinders into 1 inch pieces and flatten out each piece into small discs or mathris.
9. Grease a baking tray with some oil and place the mathris on it.
10. Brush the mathris with ghee on top.
11. Place the tray in the preheated oven and bake for 15-20 minutes.
12. Remove from oven, brush some ghee, flip and brush some more ghee on mathris.
13. Put the tray back in the preheated oven and bake for 5-6 minutes.
14. Remove from oven and cool.
15. Arrange the mathris on a serving platter and serve or store in an airtight container to serve when required.

Classic Leftover Dal Appe
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Wondering how to give your leftover dal a makeover? We’ve got the perfect recipe for you! Transform it into crispy and flavourful 'Dal Appe' by following this simple and easy recipe.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 15-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 1½ cups leftover dal ¼ cup blanched and crushed green peas ¼ cup grated carrots
• 1 cup semolina (suji)
• 3 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves 2-
• 3 tablespoons yogurt ½ teaspoon baking soda Ghee as required Coconut chutney to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take leftover dal in a bowl.
2. Add green peas, carrots, semolina, green chillies, salt, coriander, yogurt and 1 cup water and stir until combined.
3. Set aside for 10-15 minutes.
4. Add baking soda and stir until combined.
5. Warm a paniyaram pan and drizzle ghee.
6. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
7. Put a spoonful of batter in each dent, cover and cook for 5-6 minutes.
8. Flip and cook for 5-6 minutes more.
9. Serve warm with coconut chutney.

Everyday Leftover Rice Muthia
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A flavourful snack using leftover rice, gram flour and a few Indian spices to enjoy during your chai-time. This is

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 2 cups leftover cooked rice ½ cup gram flour (besan)
• 1 teaspoon sugar ¼ cup yogurt A pinch of baking soda
• 2 teaspoons oil
• 3 tablespoons whole wheat flour Tempering
• 1 tablespoon oil
• 1 teaspoon mustard seeds
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds ½ tablespoon white sesame seeds A pinch of asafoetida (hing) 10-
• 5 curry leaves 2-
• 3 green chillies, slit
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves

Method
1. Take rice in a bowl, add gram flour, ginger-green chilli paste, coriander powder, salt, turmeric powder, chilli powder, cumin powder, sugar and yogurt and stir until combined.
2. Add baking soda and oil, stir until combined.
3. Add whole wheat flour and knead into soft dough.
4. To make muthia, grease your palms with oil, take a portion of the dough and shape into a cylinder.
5. Warm sufficient water in a steamer.
6. Place the muthia in the steamer basket and steam for 12-15 minutes.
7. Switch the heat off and allow to cool completely.
8. Cut muthiya into 1 inch roundels.
9. Warm oil in a non-stick pan, add mustard seeds once they splutter, add cumin seeds, sesame seeds, asafoetida, curry leaves and green chillies and fry gently for a few seconds.
10. Add muthiya roundels and coriander leaves and toss well.
11. Remove the pan from the heat and transfer into a serving bowl.
12. Serve warm.

Everyday Leftover Roti Halwa
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

You wont believe that you can make such a flavourful and amazing halwa with your leftover Rotis. It is a very quick and simple recipe. This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Desserts Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet

Ingredients
• 8 leftover rotis
• 2 tablespoons ghee
• 1 cup milk ½ cup sugar ¼ teaspoon green cardamom powder A pinch of salt
• 1 tablespoon chopped almonds
• 1 tablespoon chopped cashew nuts
• 1 tablespoon chopped pistachios + for garnish

Method
1. Put the rotis in a blender jar and blend to a coarse powder.
2. Warm ghee in a non-stick pan, add the bended powder and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
3. Add milk, mix and cook for 2-3 minutes.
4. Add sugar, mix and cook for 3-4 minutes.
5. Add almonds, cashew nuts and pistachios and stir until combined.
6. Cook for 1-2 minutes.
7. Serve warm garnished with pistachios.

Everyday Leftover Upma Cutlets
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Got leftover upma? Make these stunning tikkis and enjoy a fabulous snack! Serve these cutlets hot with tomato ketchup. This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 1½ cups leftover rava upma
• 1 tablespoon oil + for shallow frying
• 1 tablespoon butter 2-
• 3 garlic cloves, chopped
• 1 green chilli, chopped
• 2 spring onion bulbs, finely chopped with the greens ½ cup chopped cabbage
• 1 medium carrot, chopped
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves ½ lemon ¼ cup bread crumbs Tomato ketchup to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm oil and butter in a non-stick pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add garlic and green chilli and fry gently for a few seconds.
4. Add chopped spring onion bulbs and fry gently for a few seconds.
5. Add cabbage, carrot, capsicum and a pinch of salt and fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
6. Add crushed black peppercorns, chaat masala, and red chilli flakes and roasted cumin powder and stir until combined.
7. Remove the pan from the heat and allow this mixture to cool slightly.
8. Take the leftover upma in a large bowl.
9. Add the vegetable mixture, coriander leaves, spring onion greens and squeeze the juice of lemon.
10. Add bread crumbs and stir until combined.
11. Take a portion of the mixture and shape it into a ball and gently flatten it to make triangle shaped cutlets.
12. Warm sufficient oil in a shallow pan.
13. Place the cutlets and cook for 1-2 minutes on each side till golden brown and crisp.
14. Drain on an absorbent paper.
15. Serve warm with tomato ketchup.

• 6 dried Kashmiri red chillies
• 3 tablespoons tomato ketchup
• 3 tablespoons honey 3-
• 4 stalks of spring onion greens
• 1 teaspoon vinegar
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method

11. Add fried lotus stems to the sauce and toss.
12. Add vinegar and mix.

Wholesome Malai Broccoli
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Broccoli florets marinated with fresh cream and some spices and cooked on the barbecue

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 0 large broccoli florets, blanched
• 2 tablespoons ginger-garlic paste
• 2 teaspoons mustard sauce 2-
• 3 tablespoons milk Salad to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take ginger-garlic paste, fresh cream and salt in a large bowl and stir until combined.
2. Add crushed black peppercorns and stir until combined.
3. Squeeze the juice of ½ lemon and stir until combined.
4. Add broccoli pieces and stir until combined so that all the broccoli florets are well coated with the marinade.
5. Light sufficient charcoal in a barbeque, meanwhile, skewer the broccoli pieces on skewers and place them on the barbeque grill and cook for 10-12 minutes.
6. To make the dressing, take cream cheese, garlic, crushed black peppercorns and mustard sauce in a bowl and stir until combined.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Masala Aloo Kulcha + Tamatar ki Relish
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Kulchas stuffed with a spicy potato mixture and cooked and served with a pungent tomato chutney – absolutely flavourful

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup whole wheat flour (atta) ½ cup refined flour (maida) + for dusting
• 2 medium potatoes, boiled and peeled
• 1 tablespoon coriander seeds
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds (jeera)
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger 2-
• 3 green chillies, finely chopped
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 teaspoon dried mango powder (amchur)
• 1 medium onion, finely chopped
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped fresh mint leaves
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder
• 1 tablespoon oilTamatar ki chutney
• 2 medium tomatoes (tamatar), finely chopped
• 3 tablespoons tomato ketchup
• 1 teaspoon chaat masala Juice of ½ lemon
• 2 tablespoons finely chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 2 teaspoons sugar
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Mix together wheat flour, refined flour, carom seeds, salt and 2 tablespoons oil in a parat.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add water as required and knead a soft dough.
4. Add remaining oil, knead and set aside.
5. To make the filling, dry roast coriander seeds and cumin seeds till fragrant.
6. Crush in a mortar with pestle to a coarse powder and put in a bowl.
7. Add ginger, green chillies, chilli powder, dried mango powder, onion, coriander leaves, mint leaves, potatoes and garam masala powder.
8. Mash and stir until combined.
9. Add oil and mix again.
10. To make the tamatar ki chutney, mix together tomatoes, tomato ketchup, chilli powder, dried mango powder, chaat masala, lemon juice, coriander leaves and sugar in a bowl.
11. Place an iron kadai upside down on heat.
12. Divide the dough into equal portions and brush with some oil.
13. Stuff each with a portion of the filling and shape into balls.
14. Dust the worktop with some refined and sprinkle some coriander leaves on it.
15. Place the stuffed balls on it and flatten them into discs.
16. Apply some oil on the inverted kadai, stick the kulchas on it and roast till fully done, basting with oil.
17. Serve warm with tamatar ki chutney.

Fresh Masala Rawa Puri
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

These super crisp puris are made with a dough of rawa and some spice powders

Cuisine: Maharashtrian Course: Breads Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup Fine semolina (barik rawa)
• 2 teaspoons Ginger-green chilli paste
• 1 teaspoon Red chilli powder
• 1 teaspoon Coriander powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Cumin powder 1/
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Put the semolina in a mixer jar and grind to a fine powder.
2. Spoon into a large bowl.
3. Add ginger-green chilli paste, red chilli powder, coriander powder, cumin powder, turmeric powder and salt and stir until combined.
4. Add ½ cup water and knead into soft dough.
5. Cover with a damp muslin cloth and set aside for 10-15 minutes.
6. Grease the worktop with some oil.
7. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
8. To make puris, divide the dough into small equal portions and shape them into balls.
9. Roll out each ball, on the greased worktop, into a small thin disc.
10. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
11. Gently slide the discs, one at a time, into the hot oil and deep fry till they puff up and turn crisp.
12. Drain on absorbent paper.
13. Arrange them on a serving platter and serve warm with chai.

Homestyle Mawa Gujiya
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

These gujiyas stuffed with a rich stuffing is a must at Holi celebrations.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Mithais Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 2 cups Khoya/mawa grated
• 2 tablespoons Castor sugar
• 1 tablespoon Walnuts chopped
• 1 tablespoon Cashewnuts chopped
• 1 tablespoon Dried figs chopped
• 1 tablespoon Almonds chopped
• 1 tablespoon Dates chopped
• 2 cups Refined flour (maida)
• 3 tablespoons Ghee to deep fry Oil

Method
1. Warm a non-stick pan.
2. Add khoya and castor sugar, mix and cook until khoya melts.
3. Add walnuts, cashewnuts, figs, almonds and dates, stir until combined and cook for a minute.
4. Remove from heat and set aside to cool.
5. Knead together flour and 3 tablespoons ghee with sufficient cold water to a stiff dough.
6. Divide the dough into equal portions, grease with some ghee and roll out into small discs.
7. Stuff each disc with a portion of khoya mixture and shape into gujiyas.
8. Warm sufficient ghee in a kadai.
9. Deep-fry gujiyas on medium heat till golden brown.
10. Drain on an absorbent paper.
11. Serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Mawa Muffin with Shrikhand Frosting Delight
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A jolly good fusion of two different cuisines. The little ones are going to gobble up these muffins within no time

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Desserts Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 0 grams Mawa / khoya, grated
• 0 grams Refined flour (maida)
• 0 grams Butter, softened
• 0 grams Sweet condensed milk 1/
• 2 teaspoon Baking powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Baking soda
• 5 millilitres Milk
• 1 teaspoon Vanilla essence as required Kesar shrikhand to garnish Sutarfeni to garnish Wafer biscuits to garnish Chocolate chips to garnish Small carrot triangles, blanched

Method
1. Heat the oven to 180° C.
2. Take butter in a bowl and cream it lightly with an electric hand blender.
3. Add khoya and continue to cream with the hand blender at a low speed.
4. Add condensed milk and stir until combined.
5. Sift together refined flour, baking powder and baking soda into another bowl.
6. Gradually add this flour mixture to the butter mixture and mix in cut and fold method ensuring there are no lumps formed.
7. Add milk and vanilla essence and continue to mix.
8. Put this mixture into muffin moulds till they are ¾ full.
9. Keep the moulds on a baking tray.
10. Keep the tray in the preheated oven and bake for 20-25 minutes.
11. Remove the tray from the oven and let them cool down to room temperature.
12. Remove the muffins from the moulds and keep them on a serving plate.
13. Put the shrikhand in a piping bag and pipe out over each muffins.
14. Garnish each muffin with sutarfeni, a wafer biscuit, chocolate chips and carrot triangles and serve.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Wholesome Mawa Wale Aloo
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A rich potato dish fit for a royal banquet. Rich gravy made from mawa, tomatoes and Indian spices and cooked with potatoes.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Prep: 21-25 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup grated mawa + to garnish
• 4 medium potatoes, boiled and peeled
• 1 tablespoon oil
• 1 tbsp +
• 1 teaspoon ghee 1/
• 4 cup raisins 1/
• 4 cup cashewnuts, roughly chopped
• 2 medium onions, finely chopped
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped garlic
• 1 1/
• 2 teaspoons red chilli powder
• 2 tablespoons coriander powder
• 1 tablespoon garam masala powder
• 1 teaspoon cumin powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon turmeric powder
• 3 tablespoons roughly crushed cashewnuts
• 1 cup milk 2-
• 3 green chillies, finely chopped
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm oil and 2 tbsps ghee in a deep non-stick pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Cut the potatoes into thick slices, add to the pan and cook on medium heat shallow-fry, turning sides, till potatoes turn light golden brown on both sides.
4. Drain into a bowl and set aside.
5. Add raisins to the same pan, fry gently for a few seconds.
6. Drain into another bowl.
7. Add ¼ cup roughly chopped cashewnuts to the same pan and fry gently till lightly brown.
8. Drain into a bowl.
9. Add onions, ginger and garlic to the same pan and fry gently till onions turn golden brown.
10. Add tomatoes, salt, red chilli powder, coriander powder, garam masala powder and cumin powder and stir until combined.
11. Add turmeric powder and crushed cashewnuts, mix and cook until tomatoes are pulpy.
12. Switch off heat and transfer the mixture into a blender and ½ cup milk and blend to a fine paste.
13. Switch on heat, add remaining ghee and green chillies to the same pan.
14. Add remaining milk to the ground mixture and add the mixture to the pan.
15. Rinse the blender jar with ½ cup water and add to the remaining gravy.
16. Add ½ cup water to the gravy and stir until combined.
17. Add the fried potatoes, half the fried cashewnuts, 1 tbsp fried raisins and mawa, mix and cook on medium heat for 4-5 minutes.
18. Spoon into a serving plate, garnish with mawa, remaining fried cashewnuts and remaining fried raisins.
19. Serve warm.

Methi Tamatar ki Subzi
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A healthy meal – fenugreek and tomatoes cooked with tomatoes, Indian spices and served with missi rotis.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 20-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 Large bunch fenugreek leaves (methi), cleaned 2-
• 3 medium tomatoes, finely chopped
• 2 tbsps mustard oil + for drizzling 1/
• 2 teaspoon asafoetida
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds
• 1 tablespoon chopped ginger
• 1 tablespoon chopped garlic 2-
• 3 green chillies, finely chopped
• 2 medium onions, finely chopped
• 1 tablespoon coriander powder
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder 1/
• 1 tablespoon lemon juice Fresh mint leaves to garnish

Method
1. Warm kachchi ghani mustard oil in a metal kadai till it begins to smoke.
2. Cool it slightly, add asafoetida, cumin seeds, ginger and garlic, mix and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
3. Add green chillies and onions, mix and fry gently till onions turn translucent.
4. Add fenugreek leaves and stir until combined.
5. Add coriander powder, red chilli powder, turmeric powder and salt, mix and fry gently for 4-5 minutes.
6. Add tomatoes, mash and cook until it turns soft and pulpy.
7. Divide the dough into equal portions and roll out into medium size discs or rotis dusting with flour.
8. Warm a non-stick tawa.
9. Place the discs on it, one at a time, and roast till well browned and evenly cooked from both the sides.
10. Keep them on a serving platter.
11. Add lemon juice to the sabzi and stir until combined.
12. Drizzle some mustard oil on top.
13. Brush some mustard oil on the missi rotis Transfer sabzi into a serving bowl, garnish with mint leaves and serve warm with missi rotis.

Comfort Methi Thalipeeth
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A nutritious Indian flatbread made with fenugreek leaves and a blend of spices, cooked to perfection on a griddle, offering a satisfying burst of flavors in every bite.

Cuisine: Maharashtrian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• ½ medium bunch fenugreek leaves (methi), chopped
• 1 medium onion, chopped ½ teaspoon turmeric powder Ghee as required Yogurt to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take fenugreek leaves in a bowl, add salt and set aside.
2. Take bhajnee flour, onion, turmeric powder and salt in a mixing bowl.
3. Squeeze water from the methi leaves and add to the bhajnee mixture.
4. Add sufficient water and knead to medium soft dough.
5. Place a plastic sheet on a worktop and grease it with ghee.
6. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
7. Take a big portion of mixture and place it on the sheet.
8. Apply ghee on another sheet, place it over the mixture and flatten the mixture.
9. Make more thalipeeth with the remaining mixture in the same way.
10. Warm a non-stick tawa.
11. Place a star shaped cookie cutter over the thalipeeth and cut out stars.
12. Gently remove the cut outs and place them on the heated tawa.
13. Make a cavity in centre of each star.
14. Pour little ghee into the cavities and shallow fry, turning sides, till crisp.
15. Serve warm with yogurt.

Creamy Millet Phirni
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is the recipe for the classic sweet pudding with a healthy twist - Black Raisin & Creamy Millet Phirni, Yumm! This is

Cuisine: Indian Course: Desserts Cook: 20-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet

Ingredients
• ½ cup Premium Black Raisins + for garnish
• 4 cups milk ¼ cup barnyard millet (samo) ¼ cup sugar ½ teaspoon green cardamom powder Silver warq for garnish Blanched, peeled, and slivered pistachios for garnish

Method
1. Take Premium Black Raisins in a bowl.
2. Add 1 cup water, set aside to soak for 10-15 minutes.
3. Bring milk to a boil in a deep non-stick pan.
4. Add barnyard millet, mix and cook for 8-10 minutes while stirring in between.
5. Meanwhile, put the soaked raisins into a blender jar and blend to a fine paste.
6. Once the millet is cooked and the mixture thickens, add the blended paste and stir until combined.
7. Add sugar, mix and cook for 2-3 minutes or till sugar melts.
8. Add green cardamom powder and stir until combined.
9. Switch the heat off, transfer into individual serving earthen bowls and allow to cool.
10. Once cooled, refrigerate for 1-2 hours or till chilled.
11. Garnish with silver warq, Premium Black Raisins and slivered pistachios.

Rich in all the essential nutrients, this flavourful dish is a winner. A seasonal recipe made from spinach and mustard leaves.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Main Prep: 21-25 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 2 medium bunches fresh spinach leaves, shredded
• 2 medium bunches fresh mustard leaves, shredded
• 1 medium bunch fresh bathua leves, roughly chopped
• 1 medium bunch fresh fenugreek leaves, shredded
• 2 cups soya chunks, soaked and boiled

• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped garlic 2-
• 3 green chillies, finely chopped

• 1 1/
• 2 tablespoons coriander powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon turmeric powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon cumin powder
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder + to sprinkle
• 1 teaspoon lemon juice Cottage cheese (paneer), grated to garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm soya oil in a metal kadai, add cumin seeds and fry gently till fragrant.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add ginger, garlic and fry gently till garlic turns light brown.
4. Add green chillies and onions, mix and fry gently till onions turn light brown.
5. Add tomatoes and mix.
6. Add salt, stir until combined and cook until tomatoes turn soft and pulpy.
7. Add coriander powder, turmeric powder, cumin powder, red chilli powder and garam masala powder, mix and cook until the oil separates.
8. Add spinach leaves, mustard leaves, bathua leaves and fenugreek leaves, stir until combined and cook until the leaves wilt.
9. Transfer half the cooked saag into a blender jar leaving the remaining in the kadai, blend into a fine paste and add back to the kadai.
10. Stir until combined.
11. Add water as required stir until combined.
12. Add soya chunks, stir until combined and cook on low heat for 6-8 minutes.
13. Add lemon juice and stir until combined.
14. Spoon into a serving bowl, garnish with cottage cheese, sprinkle some garam masala powder on top and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Kitchen Moongphalli ka Halwa
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Halwas can be made variously and this one is as tasty as the rest of them

Cuisine: Indian Course: Mithais Prep: 0-5 minutes Cook: 16-20 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 4 cup Peanuts (moongphalli) 3/
• 4 cup Peanut butter 1/
• 2 cup Ghee 1/
• 4 cup Semolina (suji/rawa) 1/
• 2 cup Jaggery, grated
• 1 teaspoon Green cardamom powder a pinch Saffron strands
• 1 cup Khoya (mawa)

Method
1. Warm ghee in a non-stick pan, add semolina and fry gently till fragrant.
2. Meanwhile heat another non-stick pan, add some water, jaggery and green cardamom powder, mix and cook, stirring, till jaggery melts.
3. Add peanuts and peanut butter to the semolina and stir until combined.
4. Add jaggery water and cook until the mixture thicken, stirring continuously.
5. Add saffron and mix.
6. Add khoya and mix till it melts.
7. Spoon into a serving bowl and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Mughal Sarai Dal Kachori Favourite
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Deep fried spicy moong dal stuffed kachoris served with a tangy chutney

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 1.30-2 hour Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup split skinless green gram (dhuli moong dal), soaked for
• 1 hour and boiled with turmeric powder and salt
• 0 grams readymade kachori dough
• 2 tablespoons oil + for deep-frying
• 1 teaspoon cumin seeds (jeera)
• 1 teaspoon carom seeds (ajwain)
• 1 teaspoon fennel seeds (saunf)
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped ginger ¼ teaspoon turmeric powder
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder 1½ tablespoons coriander powder
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder 1½ teaspoons dried mango powder (amchur)
• 1 small potato, boiled, peeled and mashed
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 3 tablespoons tamarind paste
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder ½ cup sugar
• 1 small potato, boiled and finely chopped
• 1 small onion, finely choppedA few fresh coriander sprigs
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. To make filling, heat 2 tablespoons oil in a non-stick pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add cumin seeds and fry gently till fragrant.
4. Add carom seeds, fennel seeds and ginger, mix and fry gently for 1-2 minutes.
5. Add green gram, turmeric powder, red chilli powder, coriander powder, garam masala powder and dried mango powder, stir until combined and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
6. Spoon into a bowl.
7. Add potato, onion and salt and stir until combined.
8. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
9. Divide the kachori dough into equal portions and roll out into small discs.
10. Place a spoonful of filling in the centre and bring the edges together to make a potli.
11. Flatten a little into kachoris.
12. Slide the kachoris, a few at a time, into hot oil and deep-fry till golden brown.
13. Drain on absorbent paper.
14. To make chutney, heat oil on a non-stick pan, add fennel seeds, cumin seeds, carom seeds and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
15. Add tamarind paste and mix.
16. Add 1½ cups water, red chilli powder, garam masala powder and sugar.
17. Mix, bring to a boil and strain into a bowl.
18. Add potato and onion and stir until combined.
19. Chop coriander sprigs and add.
20. Crack the kachoris from centre, fill in the prepared chutney, arrange them on a serving plate and serve.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Multi Grain Dal Dhokli Twist
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A popular Gujarati one meal dish made healthier by using multi grain flour

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Dals and Kadhis Prep: 41-50 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup Multi grain flour
• 1 cup Split pigeon pes (tuvar dal), soaked for 1/
• 2 tsp + pinches Turmeric powder 1/
• 2 cup Raw peanuts
• 2 tablespoons Gram flour (besan)
• 1 pinch Red chilli powder
• 1 tablespoon Ghee 1/
• 2 teaspoon Mustard seeds 1/
• 4 teaspoon Asafoetida (hing) 1/
• 2 teaspoon Fenugreek seeds (methi dana)
• 6 Curry leaves
• 2 Dried red chillies
• 1 1/
• 2 tablespoons Dal tadka masala
• 1 teaspoon Jagger, chopped
• 1 tablespoon Fresh coriander leaves, chopped to serve Khakras

Method
1. Warm a pressure cooker, add split pigeon peas, salt, ½ tsp turmeric powder, peanuts and 2 cups water.
2. Cover the cooker and cook under pressure till 4-5 whistles are released.
3. Meanwhile take multigrain flour, gram flour, salt, remaining turmeric powder and red chilli powder in a bowl and stir until combined.
4. Add sufficient water and knead into stiff dough.
5. Cover with a damp muslin cloth and set aside for 10-15 minutes.
6. Take small portions of dough and roll them on back of a fork to shape into gnocchis.
7. Uncover the cooker when the pressure settles completely.
8. Warm ghee in a deep non-stick pan, add mustard seeds and let them splutter.
9. Add asafoetida, fenugreek seeds, curry leaves, dried red chillies and the cooked pigeon peas.
10. Stir until combined for 1 minute on medium heat.
11. Add dal tadka masala and stir until combined.
12. Add sufficient water to get the required consistency and stir until combined.
13. Add the gnocchis and stir until combined.
14. Cover and cook on medium heat for 15-20 minutes.
15. Adjust salt, add jaggery and coriander leaves and mix till jaggery dissolves.
16. Switch the heat off.
17. Put ladles full of cooked dal dhokli into serving plates and serve warm with khakras.

Wholesome Mushroom Walnut Sliders
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Looking for a meatless yet incredibly satisfying meal? Look no further! These sliders are packed with umami goodness and the goodness of Walnuts.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Snacks and Starters Cook: 10-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Ingredients
• 25-
• 0 button mushrooms, chopped 10-
• 5 walnuts
• 8 toasted slider buns
• 2 tablespoons oil + for shallow frying 1½ tablespoons chopped garlic
• 1 medium onion, chopped 2-
• 1 teaspoon Worcestershire sauce ½ cup dried breadcrumbs Mayonnaise as required Lettuce leaves as required Pickled purple cabbage as required Tomato slices as required Cheese slices as required
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Transfer walnuts in a chopper and crush coarsely.
2. Warm oil in a small nonstick pan.
3. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
4. Add garlic, onion, mushrooms, crushed walnuts, thyme sprigs and fry gently for 4-5 minutes.
5. Add crushed black peppercorns, salt, paprika powder, Worcestershire sauce and cook for 2-3 minutes.
6. Allow to cool.
7. Add dried breadcrumbs and stir until combined.
8. Divide the mixture into equal portions and shape into patties.
9. Warm sufficient oil in a shallow nonstick pan and shallow fry the patties till golden brown and crisp on both the sides.
10. Arrange the lower halves of sliders buns on serving plate, spread mayonnaise and arrange lettuce leaves on top.
11. Top up with pickled purple cabbage, tomato slices and cheese slices on top.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Spiced Nachni Idiappam
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Finger millet or nachni is very high in proteins and very good for growing children. When the idiappams resemble nests, the children will simply devour them without complaint

Cuisine: South Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 51-60 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Boil 1 cup water in a deep non-stick pan.
2. Add ghee and salt and stir until combined.
3. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
4. Add nachni flour and stir until combined, stirring continuously till the mixture is smooth with no lumps.
5. Cover and cook on low heat for 2-3 minutes till mixture thickens and forms soft dough.
6. Grease the inner portion of chakli press with ghee.
7. Transfer the cooked mixture into a bowl and spread it to let it cool down.
8. Grease your palms with ghee and knead the dough lightly.
9. Divide the dough into 2 portions.
10. Shape a portion into a cylinder and put it into the chakli press fitted with sev attachment.
11. Warm sufficient water in a steamer.
12. Grease the idli moulds with ghee.
13. Press the dough through the chakli press into greased moulds.
14. Place the idli moulds in the steamer and steam for 8-10 minutes.
15. Pour chutney in center of each idiyappam, and place the tempered potatoes in the centre of the nest.
16. Serve at once.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Kitchen Nargisi Kofta
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Golden-fried hard-boiled eggs enveloped in a flavorful spiced meat mixture, served in a rich and aromatic gravy.

Cuisine: Moghlai Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 26-30 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 cup minced mutton (keema)
• 2 white bread slices
• 1 teaspoon chopped green chillies 1½ tablespoons chopped fresh mint leaves
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves
• 1 teaspoon coriander powder
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder
• 1 teaspoon crushed black peppercorns
• 1 teaspoon roasted cumin powder
• 3 eggs, hard boiled and peeled 1½ cups breadcrumbs Oil to deep fry Lettuce leaves for garnish Halved red cherry tomatoes for garnish Whole red cherry tomato for garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Put mutton in a blender jar.
2. Trim the edges and break the bread slices into small pieces and add to the blender jar along with green chilli, mint leaves, coriander leaves, coriander powder, red chilli powder, crushed black peppercorns, roasted cumin powder, garam masala powder, ginger -garlic paste and salt and blend to a fine paste.
3. Transfer the blended mixture into a bowl.
4. Break eggs into a separate bowl and beat lightly.
5. Take breadcrumbs in another bowl.
6. Take a portion of blended mixture on your palm, place a boiled egg on it and completely coat the egg with the mixture to make kofta.
7. Grease your hands with little water and smoothen the outer layer.
8. Coat koftas lightly with breadcrumbs and rub off excess breadcrumbs.
9. Dip them in egg mixture and coat them with breadcrumbs again and shake off the excess breadcrumbs.
10. Set aside.
11. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai, slide in the koftas and deep fry on medium heat till golden brown.
12. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
13. Drain on an absorbent paper.
14. Spread the lettuce leaves on a serving plate, add halved red cherry tomatoes and arrange nargisi koftas over them and place a whole red cherry tomato in center.
15. Serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Simple Neyyappam
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A traditional sweet from Kerala – it is a sweet deep-fried rice and jaggery dumpling.

Cuisine: Kerala Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 5-6 hour Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup raw rice, washed and soaked for 4-
• 5 hours
• 2 tablespoons refined flour 1½ cups chopped jaggery
• 2 teaspoons ghee ⅓ cup coconut slices
• 1 tablespoon black sesame seeds
• 1 teaspoon green cardamom powder ½ teaspoon cumin seeds ½ teaspoon dried ginger powder ¼ teaspoon nutmeg powder A pinch of salt ½ teaspoon soda bicarbonate Oil for deep frying
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm a non-stick pan, add jaggery and ½ cup water and cook, stirring till a thick syrup is formed.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Remove the impurities and set aside to cool down.
4. Warm ghee in another non-stick pan, add coconut slices and saute till light brown.
5. Drain rice and put it in a blender jar, add jaggery syrup and grind to a fine paste.
6. Spoon into a mixing bowl, add refined flour and stir until combined.
7. Add fried coconut slices, sesame seeds, cardamom powder, cumin seeds, ginger powder, nutmeg powder, salt and soda bicarbonate, stir until combined.
8. Let the batter rest for 1 hour.
9. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai, pour a ladleful of batter and deep fry on a medium low heat till golden brown in colour.
10. Drain on an absorbent paper.
11. Make more neyyappam with the remaining batter.
12. Arrange them on a serving platter and serve warm with tea.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Nimboo Mirch Wale Chane Edition
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

An excellent starter – marinated boiled chickpeas batter fried and sautéed with a spicy mixture .

Cuisine: Punjabi Course: Main Prep: 7-8 hours Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 2 cups chickpeas (kabuli chane), soaked overnight and boiled
• 1 lemon
• 2 1/
• 2 teaspoons chaat masala
• 3 tablespoons Punjabi chole masala
• 2 tablespoons oil + to deep fry
• 1 1/
• 2 cups refined flour (maida)
• 1 cup cornflour
• 1 pinch baking soda
• 2 tablespoons finely chopped garlic
• 2 green chillies, finely chopped
• 1 inch ginger, cut into thin strips
• 1 teaspoon crushed black peppercorns 1/
• 4 bunch fresh coriander leaves Green capsicum, thinly sliced to garnish Red capsicum, thinly sliced to garnish
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take chickpeas in a bowl.
2. Add 1½ tsps red chilli powder, salt, 1 tsp chaat masala and 2 tbsps Punjabi chole masala and stir until combined.
3. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai.
4. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
5. Take refined flour, cornflour, baking soda, salt and 1 tbsp oil in another bowl.
6. Add little water and stir until combined to make a thick batter.
7. Take 1 cup water in another bowl.
8. Add marinated chickpeas to the batter and stir until combined.
9. Dampen your fingers with water and pick each chickpea along with batter and slide into the hot oil.
10. Separate the chickpeas with a spoon and deep fry till they are half done and float on top.
11. Drain on absorbent paper.
12. Reheat the oil in kadai to high temperature.
13. Meanwhile heat 1 tbsp oil in a non-stick pan, add garlic and fry gently till lightly brown.
14. Add green chillies, ginger strips and fry gently till the mixture turns a light brown.
15. Add the half fried chickpeas back in kadai, and deep fry till golden and crisp.
16. Drain on absorbent paper.
17. Add chickpeas to the pan.
18. Add remaining red chilli powder, remaining Punjabi chole masala, remaining chaat masala, crushed black peppercorns and lemon juice.
19. Chop coriander leaves and add, stir until combined and cook on medium heat for 1-2 minutes.
20. Spoon into a serving bowl.
21. Garnish with green and red capsicum slices and serve warm.

Kitchen Noon Chai Kashmiri
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A warm hug from the valley in every sip, blending tradition and comfort seamlessly.

Cuisine: Kashmiri Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: Preparation Time Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 tablespoon regular tea leaves A pinch of baking soda Sugar as required 1½ cups milk ½ teaspoon salt Salted jeera biscuits to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Mix tea leaves and baking soda in a bowl.
2. Boil 2 cups water in a small stainless steel saucepan, add the tea leaves mixture.
3. Constantly lift ladle full of mixture and pour it back into the pan on medium heat till colour changes.
4. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
5. Add sugar as required and bring the mixture to a boil.
6. Add milk and again lift ladle full of mixture and pour it back into the pan till colour changes.
7. Add salt and again lift ladle full of mixture and pour it back into the pan.
8. Strain the tea into serving cups and serve warm with salted jeera biscuit.

Kitchen Oats Thalipeeth
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

One of the most popular breakfast snacks amongst the Maharashtrian, this thalipeeth has oats flour replacing the traditional bhajanee flour

Cuisine: Maharashtrian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1½ cups oats flour
• 1 small carrot, grated
• 1 large onion, finely chopped
• 2 tablespoons chopped fresh coriander leaves ½ teaspoon cumin powder ½ teaspoon coriander powder
• 3 green chillies, chopped Oil for drizzling Green chutney to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Take oats flour in a bowl.
2. Add carrot, onion, coriander leaves, cumin powder, coriander powder, red chili powder, turmeric powder, garam masala powder, salt and green chillies and stir until combined.
3. Add 1 cup water, and knead into soft dough.
4. Take a non-stick tawa, add a portion of the dough, dip your fingers in water and spread it into a thick disc.
5. Make a few holes in the center.
6. Place the non-stick tawa on heat and cook for 4-5 minutes on medium heat.
7. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
8. Drizzle a little oil.
9. Flip and cook the other side for 2-3 minutes or until golden and crisp.
10. Serve warm with green chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Onion Capsicum Arachuvitta Sambhar Mix
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Arachuvitta means freshly ground and this traditional sambhar from Tamil nadu is made with freshly ground masala

Cuisine: Tamil Nadu Course: Dals and Kadhis Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 31-40 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 medium Onion, thickly sliced
• 1 medium Green capsicum, thickly sliced 1/
• 2 cup Split pigeon peas (tuvar dal), pressure cooked with ¼ tsp turmeric powder
• 1 tablespoon Oil a pinch Fenugreek seeds (methi dana) 10-
• 2 Curry leaves 1/
• 4 teaspoon Asafoetida 1/
• 2 teaspoon Turmeric powder
• 1 1/
• 1 lemon si Tamarind, soaked in
• 1 cup warm water for
• 0 minutes
• 1 tablespoon Jaggery, chopped
• 2 tablespoons Fresh coriander leaves, chopped Masala paste 1/
• 4 cup Coriander seeds
• 1 tablespoon Cumin seeds
• 2 tablespoons Split pigeon peas (tuvar dal)
• 1 tablespoon Split Bengal gram (chana dal) 1/
• 2 cup Fresh coconut, scraped 1/
• 2 teaspoon Oil 5-
• 6 Dried red chillies (pandi) Tempering
• 1 tablespoon Ghee
• 1 teaspoon Mustard seeds a pinch Asafoetida 5-
• 6 Curry leaves

Method
1. To make the masala paste, dry roast coriander seeds and cumin seeds till fragrant in a non-stick pan.
2. Move onto a plate and set aside to cool.
3. Similarly, dry roast pigeon peas and Bengal gram in the same pan till they turn golden brown.
4. Move onto the same plate and let them cool.
5. Dry roast coconut in the same pan till it turns golden brown.
6. Move onto the same plate and let it cool.
7. Warm oil in the same pan, add the dried red chillies and saute for a minute.
8. Move onto the same plate and let them cool completely.
9. Put all these ingredients into a mixer jar, add ½ cup water and blend to a semi-smooth paste.
10. To make the sambhar, heat oil in a non-stick deep pan.
11. Add fenugreek seeds and 5-6 curry leaves and fry gently for a few seconds.
12. Add the onion and fry gently till translucent.
13. Add the capsicum and fry gently till the onions turn golden brown.
14. Add turmeric powder, sambhar masala and salt and fry gently for a minute.
15. Squeeze the soaked tamarind to extract the pulp and add along with the water and stir until combined.
16. Let the mixture come to a boil.
17. Continue to cook for 10-12 minutes.
18. Add the ground paste and stir until combined.
19. Cook for 2-3 minutes.
20. Add the cooked pigeon peas and mix.
21. Add the remaining curry leaves and stir until combined.
22. Cook for 1-2 minutes.
23. Add jaggery and mix, cook until it melts.
24. Add coriander leaves and stir until combined.
25. To make the tempering, heat ghee in a small pan.
26. Add mustard seeds and let them splutter.
27. Add asafoetida and curry leaves and mix.
28. Pour this into the sambhar and stir until combined.
29. Serve warm with steamed rice.

Orange and Quinoa Toss
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

This vibrant quinoa salad combines juicy orange segments, roasted walnuts, and crisp radishes with tender cherry tomatoes and crunchy microgreens.

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Salads Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 0-5 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet & Sour Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 2 medium oranges
• 2 cups quinoa, cooked
• 1 medium onion, peeled 8-
• 0 cherry tomatoes 8-
• 0 walnuts, roasted and roughly chopped 8-
• 0 red radishes, thinly sliced 1/
• 2 cup microgreens Feta cheese to sprinkle Dressing
• 6 tablespoons olive oil
• 4 lemons
• 3 tablespoons honey
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Peel orange and separate the segments.
2. Remove seeds and halve them.
3. Thinly slice onion and gently separate the layers.
4. To make the dressing, take marmalade in a small jar, add salt, crushed black peppercorns, olive oil and mix till well combined.
5. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
6. Halve the lemons and squeeze their juice directly into the jar and stir until combined.
7. Add honey, place the lid on and shake the mixture till everything is well combined.
8. Halve the cherry tomatoes.
9. Take quinoa in a large bowl, add walnuts, onion, cherry tomatoes, red radishes, micro greens and halved orange segments.
10. Pour the dressing into it and toss lightly.
11. Transfer the salad onto a serving plate, crumble and sprinkle feta cheese on top.
12. Serve at once.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Palak Moongphalli Til Pulao Plate
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Appearance of a dish is as important as its taste and this pulao scores high on both points

Cuisine: Indian Course: Rice Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 medium bunch Fresh spinach (palak), shredded
• 1 cup Spinach puree
• 1 cup Raw peanuts (kachchi moongphalli), soaked and boiled
• 1 tablespoon White sesame seeds (til)
• 3 cups Basmati rice, cooked
• 1 tablespoon Oil
• 1 teaspoon Cumin seeds
• 1 Bay leaf 10-
• 2 Black peppercorns
• 4 medium Potatoes, peeled
• 1 tablespoon Garlic, chopped
• 1 teaspoon Red chilli powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon Turmeric powder
• 1 tablespoon Lemon juice to garnish Fresh red chilli to garnish Tomato wedges
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm oil in a deep non-stick pan, add sesame seeds and saute till light brown.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add cumin seeds, bay leaf and peppercorns, mix and fry gently for a few minutes.
4. Cut potatoes into cubes, add to the pan and mix.
5. Add garlic, mix and cook until the potatoes turn golden.
6. Add peanuts, red chilli powder, turmeric powder, garam masala powder and salt, stir until combined and cook for 1-2 minutes.
7. Add spinach leaves, mix and cook until the leaves wilt.
8. Add spinach puree, lemon juice and Fortune Biryani Rice, mix lightly and cook until rice gets heated through.
9. Spoon into a serving bowl, garnish with red chilli and tomato wedges and serve warm.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Everyday Palak Paneer
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Cottage cheese cooked in spinach-based gravy and fresh Indian spices is a household staple. Master the art of making this simple, wholesome and nutritious veg dish by following this recipe.

Cuisine: Punjabi Course: Main Course-Veg Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Medium

Ingredients
• 0 grams paneer (cottage cheese)
• 2 medium bunches of spinach
• 1 large onion
• 1 green chilli
• 1 tablespoon chopped garlic
• 2 tablespoons oil
• 4 teaspoon garam masala
• 2 tablespoons fresh cream + for garnish A large pinch of dried fenugreek leaves (kasuri methi) powder Parantha to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Finely chop onion and cut paneer into 1-inch cubes.
2. Boil sufficient water in a deep pan.
3. Add spinach leaves and blanch fir 1 minute.
4. Drain into iced cold water.
5. Drain the spinach leaves and squeeze out excess water and put into a mixer jar.
6. Add green chilli and 1/2 cup water and grind to a fine paste.
7. Warm oil in a non stick pan, add cumin seeds and let them change colour.
8. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
9. Add the chopped onions and sautè till translucent.
10. Add garlic, mix and cook for a minute.
11. Add the ground spinach paste, mix and cook for 1-2 minutes.
12. Add salt, garam masala powder, paneer cubes and fresh cream and gently mix.
13. Cook for 1 minute.
14. Add dried fenugreek leaves powder, mix and cook for 1 minute.
15. Remove the pan from the heat.
16. Garnish with fresh cream and serve warm with parantha.

Wholesome Paneer Matar Makhana
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

This vegetarian main course recipe is made with cottage cheese, green peas and lotus seeds cooked in a rich velvety gravy. You can enjoy this with hot paranthas, chapatis or rotis.

Cuisine: Indian,Fusion Course: Main Prep: 11-15 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 0 grams Paneer (cottage cheese) cut into small cubes 1/
• 4 cup Green peas boiled
• 2 cups Lotus seeds
• 5 tablespoons Ghee
• 1 Onion
• 1 Tomato
• 1 Green chilli
• 1 inch Ginger roughly chopped 4-
• 1 teaspoon Cumin seeds
• 1 teaspoon Red chilli pepper
• 1 tablespoon Coriander powder
• 1 teaspoon Cumin powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon Turmeric powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon Garam masala powder 1/
• 4 teaspoon Dried fenugreek leaves (kasoori methi) powder
• 2 tablespoons Fresh cream
• 1 tablespoon Honey for garnish Fresh coriander sprigs to serve Paranthe to serve Onion wedges to serve Green chillies to serve Lemon wedges
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm 1 tsp ghee in a non-stick pan, add lotus seeds and saute on low heat for 5-6 minutes or till crisp.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Move onto a plate and set aside.
4. Warm 2 tsps ghee in the same pan, add onion and saute till golden brown.
5. Add tomato, green chilli, ginger and garlic cloves and saute well.
6. Add salt and stir until combined.
7. Cover and cook for 5-6 minutes.
8. Set aside to cool slightly.
9. Transfer the mixture into a blender jar and add ¼ cup water and blend to a smooth paste.
10. Warm remaining ghee in the same pan, add cumin seeds and fry gently till their colour changes.
11. Add the ground paste, stir until combined, cover and cook for 5-6 minutes.
12. Add red chilli powder, coriander powder, cumin powder and turmeric powder, stir until combined and saute for a few seconds.
13. Add ¾ cup water, mix, and cook for a minute.
14. Add green peas, cottage cheese and the roasted lotus seeds and stir until combined.
15. Sprinkle garam masala powder, dried fenugreek leaves powder and fresh cream and stir until combined.
16. Add honey and mix and take the pan off the heat.
17. Spoon into a serving bowl, garnish with coriander sprigs and serve warm with paranthe, onion wedges, green chillies and lemon wedges.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Simple Paneer Parantha
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Paranthe can be stuffed with different stuffings. Paneer paranthe is one of them that is both healthy and flavourful

Cuisine: Indian Course: Breads Prep: 2-2.30 hour Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 1 litre full cream milk
• 1 cup fresh cream 1/
• 2 cup vinegar Dough, made with
• 1 1/
• 2 cups whole wheat flour 1/
• 2 cup grated processed cheese
• 1 small onion, chopped
• 2 green chillies, chopped
• 1 teaspoon red chilli powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon garam masala powder
• 2 teaspoons chaat masala 1/
• 2 teaspoon carom seeds (ajwain)
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. To make paneer heat milk in a deep non-stick pan, add cream and bring it to a boil.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add vinegar and stir until combined.
4. Keep a large sieve over a bowl and line it with a muslin cloth.
5. When the milk curdles, pour it in the sieve and strain the excess whey.
6. Gather the edges of the cloth together and put it in cold water.
7. Remove from water, keep it again in the sieve and keep a heavy weight over it.
8. Set aside for 2 hours to let all the water drain away.
9. To make the stuffing, crumble the paneer into a bowl.
10. Add cheese, onion, green chillies, red chilli powder, garam masala, chaat masala, carom seeds, fresh coriander leaves and salt and stir until combined.
11. Divide the dough into equal portions.
12. Shape into balls and press between your palms, make a cavity in the centre of each ball and add spoonful of paneer stuffing, bring the edges together and seal completely.
13. Roll them out into five inch diameter paranthe.
14. Warm a non-stick tawa, place one parantha at a time on it and cook for ½ minute.
15. Flip it and apply ghee on the cooked side.
16. Turn it over again and spread a little more ghee on the other side too.
17. Cook, turning sides, till both sides are evenly cooked and golden brown.
18. Wrap the paranthe in Freshwrapp and pack it in tiffin with yogurt packed in a small container with lid.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Fresh Pani Puri
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Pani puri is a popular Indian street food snack, crispy puri shells filled with spicy, tangy flavored water (pani), along with a mixture of mashed potatoes, chickpeas, onions, and a tamarind chutney.

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 0-5 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Tangy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• Puris as required
• 4 medium potatoes, boiled and peeled 1½ cups moong sprouts, blanched
• 1 teaspoon roasted cumin powder
• 1 teaspoon chaat masala + for sprinkling
• 2 cups ragda Fried papdis as required Sev for sprinkling Imli chutney
• 1 cup tamarind (imli), soaked in warm water for
• 0 minutes
• 1 cup deseeded dates, soaked in warm water for
• 0 minutes ¼ cup melted jaggery
• 1 small bunch fresh mint leaves (pudina) ½ small bunch fresh coriander leaves 4-
• 2 teaspoons roasted cumin powder
• 2 tablespoons pani puri masala Juice of
• 1 lemon Ice cubes as required
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. To prepare imli chutney, drain the excess water from tamarind and dates.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Strain the tamarind in a bowl and squeeze to get the pulp.
4. Grind dates alongwith some water into a smooth and thick puree.
5. Add the date puree, melted jaggery, cumin powder, chilli powder and salt to the tamarind pulp and mix.
6. Add 1 cup water and stir until combined.
7. To prepare pudine ka pani, separate the mint leaves from the sprigs.
8. Blend together coriander sprigs, mint leaves and broken green chillies alongwith some water into a smooth mixture.
9. Place in a bowl.
10. Add salt, cumin powder, chaat masala, black salt, pani puri masala, lemon juice and 4 cups water to the ground mint leaves mixture and stir until combined.
11. Add few ice cubes and stir until combined.
12. Mash the potatoes in another bowl.
13. Add moong sprouts and mix.
14. Add cumin powder, 1 teaspoon chaat masala, chilli powder, black salt and salt and stir until combined.
15. Add salted boondi to pudine ka pani and stir until combined.
16. Crack the top of each puri, put some radga, potato-moong mixture, pour some imli chutney and pudine ka pani and serve at once.
17. To prepare sukka puri, put some potato-moong mixture on fried puris, sprinkle some salt, chaat masala, black salt and sev on top and serve at once.

Comfort Papaya Parantha
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Begin your day with these healthy paranthas - an ideal breakfast snack

Cuisine: Indian Course: Breads Prep: 21-25 minutes Cook: 21-25 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 medium Raw papaya, peeled and grated
• 0 grams Whole wheat flour (atta)
• 1 medium Onion, finely choppped
• 1 tablespoon Ginger, finely chopped 2-
• 3 Green chillies, finely chopped 1/
• 4 cup Salted peanuts, coarsely crushed
• 1 teaspoon Red chilli powder
• 1 teaspoon Garam masala powder
• 1 tablespoon Sugar
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Mix together raw papaya, whole wheat flour, onion, ginger, green chillies, salted peanuts, red chilli powder, garam masala powder, sugar, lemon juice and salt in a bowl.
2. Add sufficient water and knead into a dough.
3. Warm a non-stick tawa.
4. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
5. Divide the dough into equal portions and roll out into large discs.
6. Place the paranthas on the hot tawa, one at a time, and cook, basting with oil and turning sides, till evenly browned on both the sides.
7. Cut each parantha into 4 pieces, arrange them on a serving platter and serve warm with yogurt.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Pav Bhaji Pull Apart Special
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Indulge in the ultimate comfort food hug with our Pav Bhaji Pull Apart Special – a spicy, buttery delight that'll have you coming back for more with every tear!

Cuisine: Indian Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• A set of
• 6 multigrain pavs
• 1 tablespoon oil
• 2 teaspoons butter + for brushing
• 1 tablespoon ginger-garlic paste
• 1 large tomato, finely chopped
• 1 medium green capsicum, finely chopped ½ cup grated carrot ½ cup grated cauliflower 2-
• 3 medium potatoes, boiled, peeled and mashed ¼ cup frozen green peas 1½ tablespoons pav bhaji masala
• 1 green chilli, finely chopped
• 1 teaspoon red chilli paste
• 1 teaspoon garam masala powder
• 2 teaspoons lemon juice
• 1 tablespoon finely chopped fresh coriander leaves ½ cup grated mozzarella cheese
• 2 cheese slices
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Heat the oven at 180º C.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Warm oil and 2 teaspoons butter in a non-stick pan.
4. Add onions and fry gently till translucent.
5. Add a pinch of salt and fry gently till onions turn golden brown.
6. Add ginger-garlic paste and fry gently for a while.
7. Add tomato and cook until it turns pulpy.
8. Add capsicum, carrot and cook for a while.
9. Add cauliflower, potatoes and frozen green peas.
10. Stir until combined.
11. Add salt, pav bhaji masala, green chilli, red chilli paste and garam masala powder.
12. Fry gently well.
13. Mash the mixture well.
14. Add lemon juice and coriander leaves.
15. Add little water and mash again.
16. Line a baking tray with parchment paper.
17. Take the set of pavs on a worktop and halve it horizontally.
18. Place the base layer on the tray lined with parchment paper and spread bhaji all over it.
19. Sprinkle mozzarella cheese all over and place the cheese slices.
20. Cover with top layer.
21. Brush top of pavs with butter.
22. Keep the tray in the preheated oven and bake for 4-5 minutes or till cheese melts.
23. Place on a serving wooden platter with the parchment paper.
24. Pull apart individual pav from the whole set and serve warm.

Wholesome Pesarattu
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A traditional Andhra snack made from green gram (mung bean) batter, typically served with chutney or spicy fillings."This is healthy and tasty too.

Cuisine: Andhra Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 8-10 hour Cook: 26-30 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Spicy Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 cup whole green gram (sabut moong), soaked overnight
• 2 tbsps rice, soaked overnight
• 1 green chilli
• 1 inch ginger, finely chopped 7-
• 8 curry leaves
• 0 grams cottage cheese (paneer), cut into 1½ inch cubes 2-
• 3 medium tomato, sliced Gunpowder (molaga podi) to sprinkle Tomato ketchup to serve Coconut chutney to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Blend green gram, rice, green chilli, ginger, curry leaves and coriander leaves with sufficient water to a fine paste.
2. Spoon into a bowl and set aside for 1 hour.
3. Add salt and stir until combined.
4. Warm a non-stick pan, drizzle and little oil rub with muslin cloth.
5. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
6. Pour a ladle of batter, and spread it evenly.
7. Drizzle some more oil all around.
8. Flip when the underside is golden and cook the other side till golden brown, Place paneer cubes and tomato slices alternately and sprinkle gunpowder over them.
9. Fold and remove the pesarattu from the pan onto a serving plate.
10. Serve warm with tomato ketchup and coconut chutney.

Quick Pindi Chole Gujiya
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A fusion delicacy combining the flavors of Pindi Chole with the concept of Gujiya, resulting in a savory pastry filled with spiced chickpeas, often enjoyed as a festive snack or appetizer.

Cuisine: Punjabi Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 7-8 hour Cook: 41-50 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1½ cups chickpeas (kabuli chane), soaked overnight, boiled with teabags
• 1 tablespoon ghee
• 2 medium onions, chopped
• 1 cup tomato puree
• 1 tablespoon Punjabi chole masala Whole wheat flour dough as required Oil for deep-frying Green chutney to serve
• A pinch of hing (asafoetida)

Method
1. Warm ghee in a non-stick pan.
2. Sprinkle a pinch of hing (asafoetida) and let it bloom for a few seconds.
3. Add onions and fry gently on medium heat till onions turn golden brown.
4. Add ginger-garlic paste and fry gently till fragrant.
5. Add tomato puree, stir until combined and fry gently for 3-4 minutes till oil separates.
6. Add salt and stir until combined.
7. Add chole masala, stir until combined and fry gently for 2-3 minutes.
8. Add boiled chickpeas with cooking liquor and stir until combined.
9. Cook on medium heat for 2-3 minutes.
10. Divide the dough into equal portions, apply little ghee on worktop, apply ghee on each portion and roll out into thin discs.
11. Cut out medium round discs with a cookie cutter Mash the chole slightly and cook until the mixture dries up.
12. Spoon into a plate and set aside to cool.
13. Place a little pindi chole in the centre of each disc.
14. Apply water on edges, fold into half and seal the edges and shape into gujiyas by slightly pinching the sealed edges and folding inwards.
15. Warm sufficient oil in a kadai on medium heat, slide in gujiyas, a few at a time, and deep-fry till golden brown.
16. Drain on absorbent paper.
17. Spoon into a serving basket lined with butter paper and serve warm with green chutney.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Simple Pineapple Sandesh
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A signature mithai from Bengal, sandesh can be made in different flavours – here pineapple graces it

Cuisine: Bengali Course: Mithais Prep: 31-40 minutes Cook: 0-5 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Sweet Difficulty: Easy

Ingredients
• 1 litre Milk, curdled with
• 1 tsp citric acid 7-
• 8 Canned pineapple slices, soaked in water
• 4 tablespoons Powdered sugar 1/
• 2 teaspoon Green cardamom powder to garnish Almonds, blanched, peeled and slivered to garnish Pistachios, blanched, peeled and slivered to garnish Saffron strands

Method
1. Strain the curdled milk (chenna) through a muslin cloth and squeeze out excess water completely.
2. Transfer the chenna onto a large plate and knead lightly.
3. Add powdered sugar and green cardamom powder and knead again.
4. Transfer chenna into a piping bag.
5. Drain the pineapple slices on an absorbent paper till they are completely dry and place on a serving platter.
6. Pipe out the chenna mixture on the edges of pineapple slices in floral shape.
7. Garnish each slice with almond slivers, pistachio slivers, few saffron strands and serve.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────
Classic Pink Khandvi
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

A vibrant twist on the traditional Indian snack, featuring delicate rolls made from gram flour and yogurt, infused with a hint of beetroot puree for a visually appealing and flavorful treat

Cuisine: Gujarati Course: Snacks and Starters Prep: 16-20 minutes Cook: 11-15 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild Difficulty: Moderate

Ingredients
• 4 cup Beetroot puree
• 1 cup Gram flour (besan)
• 1 tablespoon Refined flour (maida) 1/
• 2 teaspoon Turmeric powder 1/
• 2 teaspoon Red chilli powder 1/
• 3 cups Buttermilk for greasing Oil to garnish Fresh coconut, scraped to garnish Fresh coriander leaves, chopped Tempering
• 2 tablespoons Oil
• 1 teaspoon Mustard seeds 1/
• 4 teaspoon Asafoetida (hig)
• 1 sprigs Curry leaves

Method
1. Put gram flour in a bowl, add refined flour, turmeric powder, red chilli powder, ginger-green chilli paste and salt and stir until combined.
2. Add buttermilk and mix till there are no lumps.
3. Warm a deep non-stick pan.
4. Pour the batter into it and cook on medium heat, whisking continuously till the mixture thickens.
5. Grease the back side of steel thalis with some oil.
6. Add beetroot puree to the cooking mixture and stir until combined and cook further on medium heat for 1-2 minutes.
7. While the mixture is still hot spread a thin layer of the batter with a palette knife on the greased thalis and set aside to cool.
8. After the mixture cools down, sets and the colour deepens, cut the layers vertically into thick strips, and roll each strip starting from one end to another.
9. Insert coloured toothpick in center of each khandvi and arrange them on a plate.
10. To prepare the tempering, heat oil in a non-stick tempering pan.
11. Add mustard seeds and let them sputter.
12. Add asafoetida and curry leaves and stir until combined.
13. Pour the tempering over khandvis, garnish with coconut and coriander leaves and serve.

Everyday Pista Chickpea Hummus
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

Creamy, dreamy, and full of pistachio goodness - our Everyday Pista Chickpea Hummus is a must-try. Serve this with toasted sourdough bread slices. This is

Cuisine: Fusion Course: Dips Cook: 6-8 minutes Serves: 4 Taste: Mild

Method
